# Shadow Reading 双语文章与练习

以文章为学习主线：精听 → 逐段理解 → 跟读 → 学词汇用法 → 理解检查 → 复述与迁移。词汇专题和短对话为补充。

**精编内容**：40 篇、13,964 个英文词次、240 组中英对齐段落、160 个文章表达讲解、80 道理解题，以及每篇各 1 个复述与迁移任务。另保留原 20 篇历史原稿。词次不是独立单词数，也不是掌握词汇量。

精编稿为原创教学材料，人物、机构、访谈与事件均为虚构。难度为编辑提示；音频由浏览器合成，不是真人原声。原稿编号与文字保持不变，重写稿使用独立学习记录。

## 精编 21：A Morning That Did Not Go to Plan
### 计划之外的早晨

**日常生活 · 叙事 · 基础 · 约 322 词**

**学习目标**：按时间顺序复述计划如何变化，区分最迟完成与持续到某时。

原主题对应历史原稿 1；本篇为独立重写稿。

I usually leave home at a quarter to eight. The bus takes about twenty minutes, and I like to reach the office before nine. Last Tuesday, I had an early call, so I planned to leave by eight at the latest. I put my laptop by the door and made a quick breakfast.

> 我通常七点四十五分出门。公交车程大约二十分钟，我喜欢九点前到办公室。上周二我有个较早的电话会议，所以打算最晚八点出发。我把笔记本电脑放在门边，简单做了早饭。

At five to eight, I picked up my bag and hurried downstairs. Halfway to the bus stop, I reached into my coat pocket. No keys. I checked the other pocket, then my bag. They were probably on the kitchen table. I had to turn back, but the front door had already locked behind me.

> 七点五十五分，我拿起包匆忙下楼。走到公交站的半路上，我摸了摸外套口袋。没有钥匙。我检查了另一个口袋，又翻了包。钥匙大概还在厨房桌上。我不得不折返，但大门已经锁上了。

My neighbour, Rosa, keeps a spare key for me. I rang her bell and asked, "Could you give me a hand?" She was still having breakfast, but she found the spare key straight away. My keys were not on the table, though. They were beside the kettle, under a shopping list.

> 邻居罗莎替我保管着备用钥匙。我按门铃问：“能帮我一下吗？”她还在吃早饭，但马上找出了备用钥匙。不过，我的钥匙不在桌上，而在水壶旁边，被购物清单压着。

By then, I had missed my usual bus. Instead of rushing to catch another one, I called my colleague. She said I could join the first part of the meeting from home. I sat down at the kitchen table, opened my laptop and checked that the microphone worked. The call started at half past eight.

> 这时，我已经错过平常坐的那班公交车。我没有急着去赶下一班，而是给同事打了电话。她说我可以在家参加会议的前半部分。我在厨房桌边坐下，打开电脑，检查麦克风是否正常。电话会议八点半开始。

After the call, I went upstairs to return Rosa's key. She laughed when I told her where mine had been. Then she asked whether I could help her move a small table on Friday. I said yes and suggested making lunch afterwards. We had lived next to each other for a year but had never shared a meal.

> 会议结束后，我上楼归还罗莎的钥匙。我告诉她钥匙原来放在哪里，她笑了。接着她问我，周五能否帮忙搬一张小桌子。我答应了，并提议搬完后一起做午饭。我们做邻居一年了，却从没一起吃过饭。

In the end, I reached the office at ten. I still finished the report before lunch, and nobody needed an explanation of every minute of my morning. That evening, I put a small bowl beside the door. My keys now go there. Friday's lunch went into my calendar too.

> 最后，我十点到达办公室，仍然在午饭前完成了报告，也没有人需要我逐分钟解释这个早晨。那天晚上，我在门边放了一个小碗。现在钥匙都放在那里。周五的午饭也记进了日历。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| by eight | 最迟八点 | by 表示截止，不表示一直持续。 |
| turn back | 折返 | 描述改变行进方向。 |
| give me a hand | 帮我一下 | give someone a hand 是常用请求。 |
| in the end | 最后；结果 | 引出事情的最终结果。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did the narrator turn back?
   - A. The bus was late.
   - B. The keys were still at home.
   - C. The office was closed.

2. What did the narrator agree to do on Friday?
   - A. Help Rosa move a table and share lunch.
   - B. Attend an earlier office meeting.
   - C. Buy a new kettle.

**参考答案**：1. B — 出门后发现钥匙还在家，因此折返。；2. A — 两人约好周五搬桌子，之后一起做午饭。

#### 复述
用 first、then、after that、in the end 复述早晨的四个转折，说明谁帮助了谁。

#### 换条件表达
改写成你忘带手机，并需要九点前到医院的情境。

参考表达（不唯一）：I had to be at the hospital by nine, but I left my phone at home. I turned back and asked my neighbour to help me find it.

---

## 精编 22：The Meeting We Stopped Having
### 取消一次例会之后

**工作协作 · 工作纪实 · 中级 · 约 308 词**

**学习目标**：解释一项工作安排的成本、试行办法和保留条件。

原主题对应历史原稿 2；本篇为独立重写稿。

Every morning at nine fifteen, our team stopped working and joined the same video call. We each described yesterday's work, today's plan and any problems. The meeting was supposed to last fifteen minutes. It usually did, but that did not mean it cost each person only fifteen minutes.

> 每天早上九点十五分，我们团队都会停下工作，加入同一个视频会议。每个人介绍昨天的工作、今天的计划和遇到的问题。会议原定十五分钟，通常也确实按时结束，但这不意味着每个人只付出了十五分钟。

Maya, one of our designers, explained the difficulty. She often spent the first hour drawing a complicated process. Just as she began to see how the pieces fitted together, the meeting started. Afterwards, she needed time to find her place again. The call was short; the interruption was not.

> 设计师玛雅解释了困难所在。她常用上班后的第一个小时绘制复杂流程。刚开始理清各部分关系，会议就开始了。会后，她又需要花时间恢复思路。通话时间很短，中断带来的影响却不短。

Our manager had a different concern. Without the call, how would new colleagues know whom to ask for help? A written update might be efficient for experienced staff but confusing for someone in their first week. We needed to protect focused work without making it harder to ask questions.

> 经理有另一个顾虑：没有这次会议，新同事怎么知道该向谁求助？文字更新对熟练员工可能高效，但刚入职的人可能看不明白。我们需要保护专注工作的时间，同时不能让提问变得更困难。

We agreed to try it out for two weeks. Routine updates went into a shared document by ten. Each person named any decision they needed from someone else. We also set aside twenty minutes after lunch when anyone could bring a problem. Attendance at that session was optional, not a second daily meeting.

> 我们决定试行两周。常规进展在十点前写进共享文档，每个人注明需要谁作出什么决定。另外，午饭后留出二十分钟，大家可以带着问题来讨论。这个时段自愿参加，并不是另一次全员例会。

The first week exposed a weakness: two requests disappeared in a long list of updates. We added a separate section for unanswered questions and assigned a person to each one. That made it easier to keep track of what needed a response. The document alone had not solved the problem; its structure mattered.

> 第一周就暴露出一个弱点：两个请求淹没在长长的更新列表里。于是，我们为未答复的问题单设一栏，并为每个问题指定跟进人。这样就更容易追踪哪些事情需要回应。文档本身并没有解决问题，组织方式同样重要。

At the review, Maya said the new routine no longer seemed to get in the way of her morning. A new colleague said the named contacts helped more than listening to every update. We kept meetings for decisions that required several people. We had not removed conversation; we had given it a clearer purpose.

> 复盘时，玛雅说新安排不再打断她早上的工作。一位新同事说，明确的联系人比听完所有人的更新更有帮助。需要多人共同决策时，我们仍然开会。交流还在，只是目的更明确了。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| set aside | 留出 | set aside time for something。 |
| get in the way | 妨碍 | 后面可接 of + 名词/doing。 |
| try it out | 试行 | 代词 it 放在 try 与 out 之间。 |
| keep track of | 跟踪；掌握进展 | 指持续了解，而非只看一次。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why was the daily meeting a problem for Maya?
   - A. It repeatedly interrupted a complex task.
   - B. Nobody attended it.
   - C. It lasted all afternoon.

2. What remained a reason to call a meeting?
   - A. Every minor update.
   - B. A decision involving several people.
   - C. A routine completed task.

**参考答案**：1. A — 即使会议只有十五分钟，她也需要额外时间重新进入设计任务。；2. B — 试行后仍保留需要多人共同决策时的会议。

#### 复述
说明旧安排、两种不同需求、试行规则和两周后的变化，不要只列优缺点。

#### 换条件表达
将试行周期改成一个月，并增加“紧急问题直接电话沟通”。

参考表达（不唯一）：We’ll try it out for a month. Routine updates will go in writing, but we’ll call directly if something is urgent.

---

## 精编 23：The Jacket I Returned
### 我退掉的那件夹克

**消费选择 · 叙事 · 基础 · 约 315 词**

**学习目标**：讲清购买理由、实际问题和解决办法，并比较单价与实际花费。

原主题对应历史原稿 3；本篇为独立重写稿。

I went into the shop to buy a plain blue jacket for work. Near the entrance, a sign said, "Today only: thirty percent off." Below it was a green jacket with large pockets. It was not the colour I wanted, but I stopped to look at the price.

> 我进店是想买一件上班穿的素色蓝夹克。入口附近，一块牌子写着“仅限今天：七折”。牌子下面是一件有大口袋的绿色夹克。虽然不是我想要的颜色，我还是停下来查看价格。

The original price was one hundred dollars. With the discount, I would pay seventy. I asked to try it on. The sleeves were a little long, but the assistant said I could roll them up. I looked at the sign again and imagined someone else buying the last jacket in my size.

> 原价一百美元，打折后付七十美元。我提出试穿。袖子有点长，店员说可以卷起来。我又看了看牌子，想到这个尺码的最后一件可能会被别人买走。

At home, I put the jacket on with my work trousers. The green looked different under the kitchen light. More importantly, the pockets pulled forward when I sat down. I tried to tell myself it was a good deal. Then I noticed I was already finding reasons not to wear it.

> 回家后，我穿上工作长裤，再配这件夹克。厨房灯光下，绿色看起来和店里不一样。更重要的是，坐下时口袋会向前扯。我试图说服自己买得划算，随后却发现，自己已经在找不穿它的理由了。

Luckily, I had remembered to keep the receipt. The label was still attached, and I had not worn the jacket outside. The next morning, I checked the shop's return information and took it back. The assistant inspected it, asked for the receipt and returned the seventy dollars to my card.

> 好在我记得保留收据。吊牌还在，我也没有穿出门。第二天早上，我先查了商店的退货说明，再把夹克带回去。店员检查衣服、查看收据，然后把七十美元退回我的卡里。

On the way home, I saw a simple blue jacket in another shop. It cost sixty-five dollars and was not on sale. This time, I checked the sleeves, sat down and moved my arms before deciding. It fitted properly. I did not have to change the way I wore it to make it work.

> 回家路上，我在另一家店看见一件简单的蓝夹克，售价六十五美元，没有打折。这次我先检查袖长，坐下试试，再活动双臂，之后才决定。它很合身，不需要我迁就着穿。

The blue jacket cost sixty-five dollars in total, five dollars less than the green one. That price did not include the two afternoons I had used or the extra bus journey I had paid for. Now, when I see a sale sign, I ask a different question: would I want this item if there were no discount?

> 蓝夹克总价六十五美元，比绿夹克低五美元。但这个价格没有算上我花掉的两个下午，也没算上多坐一趟公交车的费用。现在看到促销牌时，我会换个问题问自己：如果没有折扣，我还想要这件东西吗？

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| on sale | 在打折 | 此处指降价销售，不是仅仅待售。 |
| try it on | 试穿 | 代词放在 try 与 on 之间。 |
| keep the receipt | 保留收据 | receipt 的 p 不发音。 |
| in total | 总共 | 用于核对实际总花费。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did Lin buy the first jacket?
   - A. It was the colour she needed.
   - B. The discount made the decision feel urgent.
   - C. Her old jacket was broken.

2. Why did she choose the second jacket?
   - A. It fitted better and had a lower purchase price.
   - B. It had a larger discount.
   - C. It came with free shoes.

**参考答案**：1. B — 限时折扣让她觉得必须立刻决定。；2. A — 第二件虽无折扣，但价格更低且更合身。

#### 复述
用过去时复述两次购物，包含原价、折后价、退货和第二次选择。

#### 换条件表达
把夹克换成鞋，并说明折后仍比另一双贵十美元。

参考表达（不唯一）：The shoes were on sale, but they were still ten dollars more expensive than the other pair.

---

## 精编 24：A Walk That Fits into Tuesday
### 能安排进周二的一次散步

**健康生活 · 叙事 · 基础 · 约 318 词**

**学习目标**：描述一个适合具体日程的活动安排，不把个人经验当作普遍健康结论。

原主题对应历史原稿 4；本篇为独立重写稿。

In January, I joined a gym near the station. I planned to go three evenings a week on my way home. For the first two weeks, I did. Then a project became busy, my train home got later, and the gym bag stayed under my desk. I kept paying, but I rarely went.

> 一月，我加入了车站附近的健身房，打算每周三个晚上在回家路上去锻炼。前两周确实去了。后来项目忙起来，回家乘的车越来越晚，健身包就一直放在桌下。我还在付费，却很少去。

I asked myself what I could fit into an ordinary week, not an ideal one. On Tuesdays and Thursdays, I usually finished lunch with fifteen minutes to spare. There was a small park two streets from the office. I decided to walk there twice a week before making any bigger plans.

> 我开始问自己，普通的一周里能安排什么，而不是理想的一周。周二和周四，我吃完午饭通常还剩十五分钟。办公室隔两条街有个小公园。我决定先每周去走两次，再考虑更大的计划。

The route was simple. I left the office, walked past the library, went around the park and came back along the same road. It took about twelve minutes at a comfortable pace. I did not need special clothes or a booking. I only needed to stop looking at my email long enough to leave.

> 路线很简单：离开办公室，经过图书馆，绕公园一圈，再沿原路回来。以舒服的速度走，大约十二分钟。不需要特别的衣服，也不用预约，只需要暂时放下邮件，走出办公室。

My colleague Sam joined me one Thursday. At first, we talked about work the whole way. The next week, we left our phones in our pockets and noticed that a new bakery had opened. Walking with someone else made it easier to take a break instead of quietly returning to my desk.

> 一个周四，同事萨姆和我一起走。起初，我们一路都在聊工作。下一周，我们把手机留在口袋里，注意到一家新面包店开门了。有人作伴后，我更容易真正休息一下，而不是悄悄回到桌前继续干活。

We did not go out in heavy rain. On those days, I used the break to move around inside the building. If a meeting ran late, I took a shorter walk afterwards. Missing one Tuesday did not mean I had to give up the whole week or walk twice as far on Thursday.

> 大雨天我们不出门。那时，我会利用休息时间在楼里走动。如果会议结束得晚，就在会后走短一点。错过一个周二，不代表整周都要放弃，也不需要周四走两倍的路来补。

After six weeks, I was still following the routine. That was the result I could actually see. I was not measuring a dramatic change in my body, and I was not offering anyone a medical plan. I had simply found an activity that was easier to stick to than the one I had chosen before.

> 六周后，我仍然照这个安排做。这是我能实际观察到的结果。我没有测量身体是否发生巨大变化，也不是在给任何人制定医疗方案。我只是找到了一项比之前更容易坚持的活动。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| fit into | 安排进；融入 | 让活动适应已有日程。 |
| twice a week | 每周两次 | twice 表示两次。 |
| take a break | 休息一下 | take / took / taken。 |
| stick to | 坚持执行 | 描述持续遵守具体安排。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did the narrator stop using the gym?
   - A. The timetable did not fit the journey home.
   - B. The gym had no equipment.
   - C. A friend asked them to stop.

2. What made the walking plan easier to keep?
   - A. It required a new membership.
   - B. It had a specific place in the workday.
   - C. It had to be done every day.

**参考答案**：1. A — 绕路去健身房让本已很晚的回家时间更难安排。；2. B — 散步安排在午饭后，并设定了可调整的短路线。

#### 复述
比较原计划和新计划，说明场所、频率、时长和下雨时的处理。

#### 换条件表达
设计一个每周两次、每次十五分钟的语言练习安排，说明时间不够时怎么办。

参考表达（不唯一）：I’ll practise for fifteen minutes twice a week after dinner. If I’m short of time, I’ll review one short dialogue.

---

## 精编 25：The Question I Was Afraid to Ask
### 我不敢问出的那个问题

**学习成长 · 叙事 · 中级 · 约 330 词**

**学习目标**：复述学习中的具体障碍，区分“不懂概念”和“不知道下一步怎么做”。

原主题对应历史原稿 5；本篇为独立重写稿。

On the first evening of a data course, I filled three pages with notes. I recognised most of the words on the screen, so I assumed I was following the lesson. When the tutor asked us to try a short exercise, I opened the file and waited. I did not know which column to use first.

> 数据课程的第一个晚上，我记了三页笔记。屏幕上的词大多认识，于是我以为自己跟上了。老师让我们做一个小练习时，我打开文件，坐着等了一会儿。我不知道第一步该用哪一列。

Around me, people were already typing. I clicked through the instructions again, hoping to work it out without asking. The longer I waited, the more difficult it became to speak. I could imagine a useful question, but I could also imagine everyone discovering that I had not understood the previous twenty minutes.

> 周围的人已经开始打字。我再次点开说明，希望不用提问也能弄明白。等得越久，越难开口。我想到了一个有用的问题，却也想象着：所有人会因此发现，我之前二十分钟都没真正听懂。

Eventually, the person beside me whispered, "Which column did you start with?" She was stuck in exactly the same place. We asked the tutor together. Instead of saying, "I don't understand anything," I said, "I can identify the numbers, but I don't know what we're comparing. Could you show us how you choose the first column?"

> 最后，旁边的人小声问：“你先用了哪一列？”她也卡在完全相同的地方。我们一起向老师提问。我没有说“我什么都不懂”，而是说：“数字我认识，但我不知道我们在比较什么。你能演示一下怎么选择第一列吗？”

The tutor drew two small tables on paper. One showed each shop's total sales; the other showed sales per opening day. A shop that opened longer could have a larger total without selling more each day. That distinction made sense to me once I could see two actual shops instead of a page of definitions.

> 老师在纸上画了两个小表：一个列各店销售总额，另一个列每个营业日的销售额。营业天数多的店，总额可能较大，但每天不一定卖得更多。看到两家具体的店，而不是一页定义后，我理解了这个区别。

To check, she asked us to explain the example in our own words and then change one detail. I made one shop close for a week. My first answer was wrong because I forgot to adjust its opening days. Correcting that mistake taught me more than copying another explanation into my notebook.

> 为了检查理解，她让我们用自己的话解释这个例子，再改动一个条件。我让其中一家店停业一周。第一次回答错了，因为我忘了调整营业天数。纠正这个错误，比往笔记本上再抄一段解释更有帮助。

I still take notes, but I now leave space for questions. If I cannot keep up with an exercise, I try to name the exact step where I get lost. After class, I choose a different example and explain it without looking. A full notebook is easy to produce. A clear explanation takes more work.

> 我仍然记笔记，不过会留出写问题的地方。如果跟不上练习，我会尽量指出具体卡在哪一步。课后，我换一个例子，不看笔记进行解释。写满一本笔记很容易，讲清楚却需要更多努力。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| keep up with | 跟上 | keep up with a class / a conversation。 |
| work it out | 想明白；解决 | work / worked；it 放中间。 |
| in my own words（原文：in our own words） | 用我自己的话 | 不是照读原文。 |
| make sense（原文：made sense） | 说得通；有意义 | 常用 Does that make sense? |

#### 理解检查

1. What did the narrator hide during class?
   - A. That the instructions were unclear to them.
   - B. That they had already finished.
   - C. That they had missed the train.

2. What showed a better understanding afterwards?
   - A. Copying a longer definition.
   - B. Explaining a new example without notes.
   - C. Completing the original task faster than everyone.

**参考答案**：1. A — 听懂了不少术语，但不知道练习的第一步。；2. B — 换一个例子并不看笔记解释，暴露了是否真正理解。

#### 复述
说明课堂表象、实际困难、具体提问和检验理解的办法。

#### 换条件表达
用英文向老师说明：术语认识，但不明白为什么先做第二步。

参考表达（不唯一）：I recognise the terms, but I don’t understand why we do the second step first. Could you show me with a different example?

---

## 精编 26：The Train We Missed
### 错过火车的那个下午

**旅行出行 · 叙事 · 基础 · 约 322 词**

**学习目标**：比较备选行程，准确描述出发、到达和时间余量。

原主题对应历史原稿 6；本篇为独立重写稿。

We reached the station at ten past one, five minutes before our train was due to leave. I thought that was enough time. Then I saw the signs: this entrance served the local lines, and our platform was on the other side of the building. The passage between them was closed for repairs.

> 我们一点十分到车站，距离火车原定发车还有五分钟。我以为时间足够。接着看见标牌：这个入口通往本地线路，我们的站台在建筑另一边，连接两侧的通道正在维修，无法通行。

We had to go outside, walk around the station and enter again. When we found the right platform, the train was already moving. My friend Ella looked at me but did not say anything. I was holding the tickets and a printed plan for an afternoon that could no longer happen exactly as written.

> 我们只好走到外面，绕过车站再进一次。找到正确站台时，火车已经开动。朋友埃拉看着我，什么也没说。我手里拿着车票和打印好的行程表，但这个下午已经不可能完全照计划进行了。

At the information desk, we asked about the next service. There were two choices. A train at half past one required a change and would get us there at two forty. A direct train left at two and arrived at three. We needed to find out whether our tickets were valid on either service.

> 我们到服务台询问下一班车。有两个选择：一点半出发的车需要换乘，两点四十分到；直达车两点出发，三点到。我们还需要查清原来的车票是否能用于这两班车。

The assistant checked our tickets and explained the conditions. Both trains were possible without an extra payment. We chose the direct one instead of rushing through another station. We had nearly forty minutes, so we bought sandwiches and sat outside. Ella suggested dropping the museum from our plan rather than squeezing everything in.

> 工作人员核对车票并解释使用条件。两班都可以坐，不用补钱。我们选择直达车，不再赶着去另一个车站换乘。还有将近四十分钟，于是买了三明治，坐在外面。埃拉建议取消博物馆行程，而不是把所有活动硬塞进去。

Our second train left on time. When we arrived, we walked through the old town and spent an hour by the river. We missed the exhibition, but we had time to stop in a small bookshop that was not on my list. Ella found a book she had been trying to buy for months.

> 第二班车准时开了。到达后，我们穿过老城区，在河边待了一个小时。虽然错过展览，却有时间逛一家原计划里没有的小书店。埃拉找到了一本想买好几个月的书。

On the journey home, I changed my plan for our next trip. I wrote down the station entrance, not just its name, and allowed at least twenty minutes before departure. I also left one part of the afternoon empty. A useful plan could help us choose; it did not need to fill every minute.

> 回程时，我修改了下次旅行的计划：记下车站入口，而不只是站名，并在发车前至少留二十分钟。我还空出了一段下午时间。好计划可以帮助选择，却不必把每一分钟都排满。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| at least | 至少 | 表示最低数量或时长。 |
| find out | 查明；弄清 | find / found / found。 |
| instead of | 代替；而不是 | 后接名词或 doing。 |
| on time | 准时 | 不同于表示来得及的 in time。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did they miss the train?
   - A. They went to the wrong station entrance.
   - B. They took too long buying food.
   - C. The train left early.

2. Why did they choose the later direct train?
   - A. It was the earliest arrival.
   - B. It gave them time to eat without another change.
   - C. It was free.

**参考答案**：1. A — 到达了错误入口，走到正确站台时已晚了。；2. B — 他们放弃到达更早但需要换乘的路线，选择从容一点的直达车。

#### 复述
复述误会、两种备选路线、选择原因和到达后的变化。

#### 换条件表达
提出晚半小时出发但无需换乘的方案，并问同伴是否接受。

参考表达（不唯一）：We could leave half an hour later and take a direct train instead of changing. Would that work for you?

---

## 精编 27：When the App Became Another Job
### 当应用成了另一份工作

**科技与信息 · 观点叙事 · 进阶 · 约 346 词**

**学习目标**：区分工具的功能数量与实际收益，用具体证据支持判断。

原主题对应历史原稿 7；本篇为独立重写稿。

The app promised to organise my projects, reading, exercise and personal errands in one place. During the first weekend, I built coloured categories and connected several calendars. Each task could have a priority, a deadline, a label and a progress measure. I enjoyed setting it up because the screen made my scattered responsibilities look manageable.

> 这个应用承诺把项目、阅读、运动和私人事务集中管理。第一个周末，我建立了彩色分类，还关联了几个日历。每个任务都能设置优先级、截止日期、标签和进度指标。我很享受配置过程，因为屏幕让零散的责任看起来变得可控。

By the third week, I was spending part of every evening maintaining the system. If I postponed a task, I adjusted its date in two views and checked the effect on a weekly chart. Sometimes I would put off writing a difficult paragraph by reorganising the project that contained it. The system recorded activity, but not all of that activity moved the work forward.

> 第三周，我每天晚上都要花一段时间维护这套系统。推迟任务时，要在两个视图里改日期，再检查周图表的变化。有时，我通过整理某个项目，拖延写其中一段难写的文字。系统记录了活动，但这些活动并不都让工作取得进展。

A colleague asked a question that caught me off guard: "What decision can you make with that chart?" I explained what each colour meant, then realised I could not answer her question. The chart showed how many tasks I had closed. It did not show whether I had finished the one piece of work that mattered most that week.

> 一位同事的问题让我措手不及：“你能用这张图作什么决定？”我解释了每种颜色的意思，随后发现自己没回答问题。图表展示关闭了多少任务，却没说明那周最重要的一件工作是否完成。

I decided to strip it back for a fortnight. I kept a single list of the next three actions and placed project details in their existing documents. I turned off routine notifications and left reminders only for appointments with other people. At first, the simpler screen felt incomplete. There were fewer places to look busy without making a choice.

> 我决定简化系统，试行两周。只保留一张列出接下来三个行动的清单，项目细节仍放在原有文档里。我关掉常规通知，只保留与他人约定事项的提醒。最初，简洁的屏幕让我觉得不完整：不作选择就能显得忙碌的地方少了。

In practice, the smaller system was easier to maintain. It did lose something: I could no longer search a detailed history of every postponed errand. For my purposes, that was an acceptable trade-off. Another person might need the history for billing or coordination. The important question was which decisions the information supported, not whether the feature looked sophisticated.

> 实际使用中，精简后的系统更易维护。它确实失去了一些东西：我无法再检索每件延期小事的详细历史。对我的需求而言，这个取舍可以接受。其他人可能需要历史来计费或协调。关键在于信息支持什么决定，而不在于功能看起来多高级。

I still use the app, but I no longer treat complete organisation as a separate goal. A tool can reduce uncertainty, preserve a commitment or make a next step visible. When maintaining it happens at the expense of those outcomes, I now consider removing a feature before adding another one.

> 我仍然使用这个应用，但不再把“把一切整理得完美”当作独立目标。工具可以减少不确定性、保留承诺，或让下一步清晰可见。如果维护工具反而牺牲了这些结果，我现在会先考虑删掉一个功能，再考虑增加一个。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| put off | 推迟 | put off doing something。 |
| in practice | 在实际使用中 | 与理论或预期作比较。 |
| at the expense of | 以……为代价 | 说明收益伴随的损失。 |
| strip it back | 删减至必要部分 | strip back 在此指简化。 |

#### 理解检查

1. What was the central problem with the app?
   - A. It could not send notifications.
   - B. Maintaining it displaced the work it was meant to support.
   - C. It did not allow enough categories.

2. What did the narrator keep after the experiment?
   - A. Only a list of the next three actions.
   - B. Every category and daily chart.
   - C. No record of tasks at all.

**参考答案**：1. B — 整理工具逐渐占用了原本应该用于实际工作的时间。；2. A — 最终只保留下一步三个行动，细节仍在原文件中。

#### 复述
用一个具体功能说明它最初为何有吸引力、何时带来成本，以及删减后的取舍。

#### 换条件表达
向团队解释为什么不启用所有通知，但保留紧急提醒。

参考表达（不唯一）：In practice, routine notifications interrupt focused work. We’ll keep urgent alerts and review the other updates at set times.

---

## 精编 28：Dinner at Three Different Times
### 三个时间表，一顿家庭晚饭

**家庭关系 · 叙事 · 基础 · 约 317 词**

**学习目标**：描述彼此冲突的安排，提出具体可行的共同时间。

原主题对应历史原稿 8；本篇为独立重写稿。

For years, dinner at our house was at seven. My mother cooked, my father set the table, and I washed the dishes afterwards. Then my father began an evening course, my mother changed her working hours, and I started a job across the city. The old plan no longer fitted our week.

> 多年来，我们家都在七点吃晚饭。妈妈做饭，爸爸摆餐具，我饭后洗碗。后来爸爸开始上晚课，妈妈换了工作时间，我也到城市另一头上班。原来的安排不再适合我们的一周。

On Mondays, my mother came home at six, I arrived at half past seven, and my father did not return until nine. She kept waiting to see who wanted to eat. I often bought a sandwich on the way home because I did not know what was ready. By nine, someone was always tired or hungry.

> 周一，妈妈六点回家，我七点半到，爸爸九点才回来。妈妈总在等，看谁要吃饭。我常因不知道家里准备了什么，在回家路上买三明治。到了九点，总有人又累又饿。

One Sunday, we put our calendars on the table and tried to work out a different arrangement. We found that Tuesday and Thursday were especially difficult. Instead of asking everyone to wait, we agreed to cook food that was easy to share at different times. Whoever ate later could heat it up.

> 一个周日，我们把日历放到桌上，试着商量新安排。周二、周四尤其难协调。于是，我们不再让所有人互相等，而是同意做一些方便分时吃的食物。晚吃的人自己热一下。

We also decided to take turns cooking. On my evening, I made rice, vegetables and chicken, then left a note saying what was in each container. My father cooked soup on Saturdays. My mother admitted that choosing what to cook every day had become more tiring than the cooking itself.

> 我们也决定轮流做饭。轮到我时，我做了米饭、蔬菜和鸡肉，并留张纸条说明各个盒子里装的是什么。爸爸周六煮汤。妈妈说，每天决定做什么，已经比做饭本身更累了。

There was still one thing we wanted to keep: a meal with nobody rushing away. We chose Sunday at half past six. Phones stayed off the table, and the meal did not need to be special. One week, we ate eggs on toast because none of us had been shopping. We still sat together for an hour.

> 但有一件事我们仍想保留：一起吃一顿谁都不急着走的饭。我们选定周日六点半。手机不上桌，饭菜也不必特别。有一周，大家都没买菜，只吃鸡蛋配吐司，却仍坐在一起聊了一个小时。

Our kitchen is not more organised in every way. There are sometimes three containers in the fridge with three different names. But we argue less about who should have waited. We found a way to make room for one another without pretending that all our days followed the same timetable.

> 我们的厨房并不是所有方面都更整齐了：冰箱里有时放着三个写着不同名字的饭盒。但我们少了关于“谁应该等谁”的争执。我们找到了给彼此留时间的办法，也承认每个人的日程并不相同。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| work out | 商量出；解决 | work out a plan。 |
| take turns | 轮流 | take turns doing something。 |
| heat it up | 把它热一下 | 代词 it 放中间。 |
| make room for | 给……留出空间或时间 | 此处指为相处留时间。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why was dinner difficult to arrange?
   - A. Everyone disliked the same food.
   - B. The family came home at different times.
   - C. The kitchen was closed.

2. What did the family agree to do?
   - A. Eat together every evening without exception.
   - B. Protect one shared dinner and simplify the other evenings.
   - C. Stop cooking at home.

**参考答案**：1. B — 三个人的工作、课程和回家时间不同。；2. B — 他们保留周日晚饭作为固定相聚时间，其他晚上灵活安排。

#### 复述
先分别介绍三个人的时间，再说明他们怎样安排做饭和一起吃饭。

#### 换条件表达
提出每周三一起吃晚饭，其余日子轮流做饭。

参考表达（不唯一）：Could we have dinner together on Wednesdays and take turns cooking on the other days?

---

## 精编 29：What the Recipe Did Not Tell Me
### 菜谱没有告诉我的事

**饮食与烹饪 · 叙事 · 基础 · 约 329 词**

**学习目标**：根据实际情况描述步骤、判断状态，并准确表达数量变化。

原主题对应历史原稿 9；本篇为独立重写稿。

The recipe looked simple: cook an onion, add tomatoes and beans, then leave the pan on a low heat for twenty minutes. I had invited my sister for dinner and wanted to make something warm and easy. I bought all the ingredients, measured them carefully and put the instructions beside the cooker.

> 菜谱看起来很简单：先炒洋葱，加入番茄和豆子，再小火煮二十分钟。我请姐姐来吃晚饭，想做一道热乎又容易的菜。我买齐材料，仔细量好，把说明放在炉子旁边。

I tried to follow the instructions exactly. After twenty minutes, however, most of the liquid had disappeared. The beans were sticking to the bottom, and the onion tasted too strong. I turned off the heat and checked the recipe again. The numbers were right. The food in front of me did not look right at all.

> 我尽量严格照说明做。但二十分钟后，液体几乎煮干了。豆子粘在锅底，洋葱味也太冲。我关火，又看一遍菜谱。数字都没错，眼前的菜却完全不像应该有的样子。

My sister arrived while I was considering ordering a pizza. She looked at the pan and asked what kind I had used. Mine was much wider than the one in the recipe's photograph. The same amount of sauce covered a larger surface. She also noticed that my idea of a low heat was still quite hot.

> 姐姐来时，我正考虑点披萨。她看了看锅，问我用的是什么锅。我的锅比菜谱照片中的宽很多，同样多的酱汁铺在更大的面积上。她还发现，我认为的“小火”其实仍然很旺。

We added warm water a little at a time and stirred the beans away from the bottom. Then we turned down the heat and put the pan back on the cooker. Instead of watching only the clock, we watched the sauce. When it moved slowly from the spoon but still covered the beans, we stopped cooking.

> 我们每次加一点温水，把锅底的豆子轻轻搅开，接着调低火力，把锅放回炉子上。这次，我们不只看钟，也看酱汁。当它能从勺子上缓慢流下、又仍能裹住豆子时，就停止加热。

The following week, I cooked it again. I started with less water and kept some ready to add. I wrote notes beside the recipe: "onion soft, not brown" and "sauce loose enough to stir." When I made twice as much for four people, those descriptions were more useful than simply doubling the cooking time.

> 接下来一周，我又做了一次。起初少放些水，同时备好水随时添加。我在菜谱旁写下：“洋葱变软，但不要变褐色”“酱汁要能轻松搅动”。后来做四人份、分量翻倍时，这些描述比直接把烹饪时间加倍更有用。

I still use measurements. They tell me where to begin. But now I also look for instructions that explain what I should see, hear or feel as I cook. Before serving that second dinner, I tasted a spoonful. Then, for the first time that evening, I sat down instead of checking the recipe again.

> 我仍然使用量具，因为它们告诉我从哪里开始。但现在，我也会寻找能说明烹饪时应该看到、听到或感受到什么的指引。第二次上菜前，我尝了一勺。随后，那天晚上我终于坐了下来，没有再看一遍菜谱。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| follow the instructions | 按照说明做 | follow 也可指遵循步骤。 |
| a little at a time | 每次加一点 | 强调分次，而非一次加入。 |
| turn down（原文：turned down） | 调低 | turn down the heat / turn it down。 |
| twice as much | 两倍那么多 | 比较不可数数量。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did the sauce become too thick?
   - A. The narrator used a wider pan and cooked it too long.
   - B. There were no tomatoes.
   - C. The sister added too much water.

2. What did the narrator change in the written recipe?
   - A. Only the name of the dish.
   - B. Added descriptions of appearance and a smaller starting quantity.
   - C. Removed all measurements.

**参考答案**：1. A — 锅的大小和火力不同，固定时间不适用。；2. B — 补上了状态描述，并减少起始用水量，方便逐步调整。

#### 复述
描述第一次失败的具体原因，以及第二次怎样调整水、火和判断方式。

#### 换条件表达
说明做双份不意味着盐也要一开始就一次加双倍。

参考表达（不唯一）：I’m making twice as much, but I’ll add the salt a little at a time and taste it before adding more.

---

## 精编 30：Finishing Behind the Runner in Blue
### 在蓝衣跑者后面抵达终点

**运动与挑战 · 叙事 · 中级 · 约 362 词**

**学习目标**：用具体比较说明目标如何调整，并区分结果排名与过程选择。

原主题对应历史原稿 10；本篇为独立重写稿。

Before my first five-kilometre event, I had a modest plan: run the first half slowly enough to speak in full sentences, then decide whether to increase my pace. During training, I could usually manage that. On the morning of the event, the crowd, music and numbered shirts made the plan feel less important than it had at home.

> 第一次参加五公里活动前，我的计划很朴素：前半程跑得慢一点，保持还能说完整句子的速度，再决定要不要加速。训练时通常能做到。但活动当天，人群、音乐和号码布，让在家制定的计划显得没那么重要了。

A runner in a bright blue shirt started just ahead of me. I decided to keep up with him for the first few minutes. He looked relaxed, and I assumed that meant his pace would suit me. I did not know how often he trained or what distance he normally ran. I was comparing two shirts in a crowd, not two runners with the same experience.

> 一位穿亮蓝上衣的跑者从我前面起跑。我决定先跟他几分钟。他看上去很轻松，我就以为他的速度也适合我。但我不知道他训练多频繁、平常跑多远。我比较的只是人群里的两件上衣，而不是经历相同的两个跑者。

After two kilometres, I could no longer finish a sentence without taking another breath. The blue shirt moved further away. I tried to close the gap and felt worse. When I finally slowed to a walk, several people passed me. That bothered me more than the tiredness. My original aim had somehow become an obligation not to fall behind a stranger.

> 两公里后，我已经不能不换气地说完一句话。蓝色上衣越来越远。我试图缩小距离，却更难受了。终于减速走路时，好几个人超过了我。相比疲倦，这更让我在意。不知何时，最初的目标变成了“不能落后于一个陌生人”。

At the water station, a volunteer asked whether I needed help. I said I wanted a short rest. I moved to the side, drank some water and watched people choose different ways through the event. Some walked together; others ran alone. Nobody was following the private contest I had invented with the runner in blue.

> 在饮水站，志愿者问我是否需要帮助。我说想短暂休息。我站到一旁，喝了点水，看着人们以不同方式完成活动：有人结伴走，有人独自跑。没有人在意我与蓝衣跑者之间那场自己想象出来的比赛。

For the last kilometre, I chose a pace I could control. I walked when I wanted to and began running again without trying to recover every lost second. Near the finish, I saw the blue shirt outside the course. He had already finished and was cheering for someone else. He probably had no idea that I had been trying to race him.

> 最后一公里，我选择了自己能掌控的速度。想走就走，再开始跑时，也不试图追回每一个“损失”的秒数。临近终点，我在赛道外看见那件蓝衣。他已经完成，正在为别人加油。他大概完全不知道，我一直想和他比赛。

My finishing time was slower than my best training time. I wrote it down anyway, along with the decision that had made the middle of the race so difficult. For the next event, I planned to start with a friend who knew my pace. I wanted a reminder that I could slow down before I was forced to stop.

> 最终成绩比我训练时最好的一次还慢。我仍把它记下来，也记下那个让中段变得艰难的决定。下一次活动，我打算和了解我节奏的朋友一起起跑。我希望有人提醒我：不必等到被迫停下，才允许自己慢下来。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| keep up with | 跟上 | 强调维持相同速度或进展。 |
| fall behind | 落后 | fall / fell / fallen。 |
| slow down | 慢下来 | 可用于速度和工作节奏。 |
| ahead of | 在……前面 | 这里用于赛跑中的位置。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did the narrator begin too quickly?
   - A. They followed a faster runner rather than their plan.
   - B. The coach ordered them to sprint.
   - C. The course was shorter than expected.

2. What was different about the final kilometre?
   - A. The narrator deliberately chose a sustainable pace.
   - B. The narrator became the race winner.
   - C. The event was cancelled.

**参考答案**：1. A — 为了追蓝衣跑者，偏离了原先节奏。；2. A — 最后一公里主动选择可持续节奏，而非继续与某个人较劲。

#### 复述
复述赛前目标、开跑后的选择、中途困难和最后的决定，包含两个比较级。

#### 换条件表达
向同伴说明你会慢一点，但仍想跑完全程。

参考表达（不唯一）：I need to slow down, but I still want to finish the whole course. You can go ahead of me.

---

## 精编 31：The Street After the Rain
### 雨后的那条街

**城市与环境 · 观察叙事 · 中级 · 约 347 词**

**学习目标**：区分观察、猜测和后续核实，描述位置与变化。

原主题对应历史原稿 11；本篇为独立重写稿。

After a night of heavy rain, a deep puddle stretched across the pavement outside our building. People stepped into the road to get around it. A delivery worker pushed his bicycle through the water and then lifted it onto the kerb. By the time I reached the bus stop, my left shoe was wet and I was annoyed with the street rather than curious about it.

> 一夜大雨后，我们楼外的人行道上积了一大片水。行人绕到马路上通过。一位送货员推着自行车穿过积水，再抬上路沿。我走到公交站时，左脚鞋子已经湿了，只顾埋怨这条街，没有想过原因。

That afternoon, our neighbours' group filled with explanations. Someone blamed the new building across the road. Another person said the drains were simply too small. Neither had checked. Rosa suggested starting with what we could see. We took photographs from the same corner and marked the place where the water seemed deepest.

> 当天下午，邻居群里出现了各种解释。有人怪马路对面的新楼，也有人说排水管本来就太小，但谁也没检查过。罗莎建议先看能确认的事实。我们从同一个街角拍照，标出积水看起来最深的位置。

Near the crossing, leaves and pieces of packaging covered a drain. A second drain further along the pavement was clear. We reported both observations to the maintenance team rather than saying we knew the whole cause. The team removed the material and checked the opening. They explained that they still needed to inspect the pipe below.

> 路口附近，一个排水口被树叶和包装碎片覆盖。沿人行道再往前，另一个排水口则是通畅的。我们把两处观察都报告给维护人员，没有声称已经找出全部原因。工作人员清走杂物，检查入口，并说明仍需要查看地下管道。

The report received a reference number, which made it possible to follow up without retelling the story each time. A shopkeeper agreed to keep loose packaging away from the crossing. Several residents offered to photograph the street after the next heavy shower. Nobody was asked to enter the road or clear the drain themselves.

> 报告有了编号，之后跟进就不必每次从头解释。一位店主同意不再让散落的包装物留在路口。几位居民愿意在下一次大雨后再拍照。没有人被要求走进车道，或自行清理排水设施。

Two weeks later, rain left a smaller puddle in the same place. That looked encouraging, but we did not know whether the rainfall had been equally heavy. Our photographs showed an improvement in the pavement, not proof that every drainage problem had been solved. The maintenance team confirmed that their inspection was still open.

> 两周后，同一位置在雨后只留下一片较小积水，看起来有所改善。但我们不知道两次雨量是否相同。照片显示人行道情况变好了，却不能证明排水问题已经全部解决。维护团队也确认，检查还没有结束。

For the time being, I pay more attention to that corner. I notice where the street slopes and which doorway becomes difficult to reach first. The pavement is a small part of a much larger system, but it is also where people carry shopping, guide pushchairs and wait for buses. Starting with that detail gave our complaint a place, a record and a next step.

> 至少目前，我开始更留意那个街角：街面向哪里倾斜，哪一处门口最先变得难以接近。人行道只是大系统的一小部分，但也是人们提菜、推婴儿车、等公交的地方。从这个细节出发，我们的抱怨有了具体位置、记录和下一步。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| get around it | 绕过它 | get around an obstacle 绕过障碍。 |
| rather than | 而不是 | 比较两个行动选择。 |
| follow up | 跟进 | follow up on a report。 |
| for the time being | 暂时 | 表示当前安排仍可能调整。 |

#### 理解检查

1. What did the residents discover about the drain?
   - A. It had been completely removed.
   - B. It was blocked by leaves and packaging.
   - C. It served the entire city.

2. Why did they photograph the street again?
   - A. To compare conditions after similar rain.
   - B. To advertise a shop.
   - C. To prove all flooding had ended permanently.

**参考答案**：1. B — 清理前的检查发现，排水口被树叶和包装物堵住。；2. A — 他们希望比较相近降雨条件下同一处的情况，不把一次观察当作永久结论。

#### 复述
分别说明居民实际看见了什么、最初猜测什么，以及还需要检查什么。

#### 换条件表达
向管理人员报告入口积水，包含位置、时间和你能确认的情况。

参考表达（不唯一）：There was standing water outside the north entrance at eight this morning. I saw leaves covering the drain, but I don’t know whether there is another blockage underneath.

---

## 精编 32：Ten Minutes with One Painting
### 与一幅画相处十分钟

**文化与艺术 · 观察叙事 · 中级 · 约 356 词**

**学习目标**：用具体视觉细节描述作品，区分看到的内容与自己的解释。

原主题对应历史原稿 12；本篇为独立重写稿。

I used to move through art galleries as though I were trying to finish a list. I looked at a painting, read the label and moved on. If someone asked what I had enjoyed, I could usually remember a famous name but not a particular brushstroke, shadow or face. On one quiet afternoon, I tried a different approach: ten minutes with one painting.

> 以前逛美术馆，我像在完成清单：看画，读标签，再走向下一幅。若有人问喜欢什么，我通常记得某个名家的名字，却想不起具体笔触、阴影或面孔。一个安静的下午，我决定换种方式：只和一幅画相处十分钟。

I chose a modest painting of a room after a meal. At first glance, it seemed almost empty. There were two chairs, a table and a pale window. I resisted reading the label straight away. Instead, I named the colours I could see and noticed that the light on the table was warmer than the light on the floor.

> 我选了一幅并不起眼的画，内容是饭后的房间。乍看几乎空无一人：两把椅子、一张桌子、一扇颜色很浅的窗。我忍住没有马上读标签，而是先说出看到的颜色，并发现桌面的光比地板上的光更暖。

After a few minutes, I noticed a small figure in the background, close to the window. The person's face was turned away. Suddenly, the empty chairs suggested an absence rather than a tidy room. Had the other diners left? Was this person waiting for someone? The paint did not answer, and my questions were not facts about the scene.

> 几分钟后，我注意到背景里、窗边有一个小人物，脸朝着另一边。那些空椅子忽然让我想到“有人离开了”，不再只是整洁的房间。其他用餐者走了吗？这个人在等人吗？画没有回答，这些问题也不是画面已经证实的事实。

Another visitor stopped beside me. She thought the room looked peaceful. I said it seemed lonely to me. We pointed to the same details but connected them differently: the unused cup comforted her because it suggested someone would return; I had seen it as evidence that someone had gone. Neither of us needed to persuade the other.

> 另一位观众在我身边停下。她觉得房间很平静，我却觉得孤独。我们指着相同的细节，却作了不同联想：没动过的杯子让她安心，因为似乎有人还会回来；我则把它看成有人已经离去的痕迹。我们不需要说服对方。

Eventually, I read the label. It told me when the work had been made and described the artist's interest in ordinary interiors. That context helped, but it did not settle what I should make of the room. I went back to the window and began to pay attention to the narrow strip of blue around its frame.

> 最后我才读标签。它说明创作时间，也介绍画家对日常室内景象的兴趣。这些背景有帮助，却没有决定我该怎样理解这个房间。我重新看向窗户，开始留意窗框周围的一条窄蓝边。

When I left, I had seen fewer paintings than usual. Yet I could describe this one on the journey home without opening the museum's website. I remembered the chairs, the turned face and the disagreement between two viewers. Spending longer had not made me an expert. It had given me something specific to look at, remember and discuss.

> 离开时，我看过的画比平时少，但回家路上，不打开美术馆网站也能描述这一幅。我记得椅子、转开的脸，以及两位观众不同的理解。多看一会儿没有让我成为专家，却给了我可以具体观察、记忆和讨论的东西。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| at first glance | 乍看之下 | glance 指短暂一瞥。 |
| in the background | 在背景中 | 与 in the foreground 对照。 |
| pay attention to | 留意 | to 是介词。 |
| make of | 如何理解；如何评价 | What do you make of it? |

#### 理解检查

1. What did the narrator notice only after waiting?
   - A. A small figure near the window.
   - B. A price written on the frame.
   - C. A second painting behind the first.

2. How did the museum label affect the narrator?
   - A. It completely settled the painting’s meaning.
   - B. It added context without removing the questions.
   - C. It described exactly the same personal memory.

**参考答案**：1. A — 多看一会儿后才注意到窗边的小人物。；2. B — 标签补充背景，却没有替观众决定唯一解释。

#### 复述
不用 beautiful 或 interesting，描述画中三处细节，再说明你对其中一处的解释。

#### 换条件表达
描述一张照片，明确区分“看见两个人”与“猜测他们在等车”。

参考表达（不唯一）：I can see two people beside the road. I think they may be waiting for a bus, but the photograph does not show a bus stop.

---

## 精编 33：The Result That Did Not Fit Our Guess
### 没有支持猜想的结果

**科学与判断 · 实验叙事 · 进阶 · 约 382 词**

**学习目标**：清楚区分假设、比较条件、观察结果与结论边界。

原主题对应历史原稿 13；本篇为独立重写稿。

Our evening science group wanted to investigate a simple question: would an extra desk lamp make young bean plants grow taller? Before starting, we wrote down a prediction and agreed on a way to measure height. Everyone expected a visible difference. The lamp looked like a useful intervention, and it was tempting to treat the experiment as a demonstration of something we already knew.

> 我们的晚间科学小组想研究一个简单问题：多加一盏台灯，会让豆苗长得更高吗？开始前，大家写下预测，统一了测量高度的方法。所有人都期待看见差异。灯看起来像个有用的干预，因此我们很容易把实验当作演示一个“已经知道”的结论。

We placed one tray beside a window and another beneath the lamp. After a week, the plants in the second tray were taller. At the next meeting, however, someone asked who had watered them. Different people had used different amounts of water. The trays were also on different shelves. We had changed several conditions while describing only one. We had failed to account for the different watering routines.

> 我们把一盘苗放在窗边，另一盘放在台灯下。一周后，第二盘长得更高。但下次聚会时，有人问是谁浇的水。原来不同的人用了不同水量，两盘苗也放在不同架子上。我们同时改变了好几个条件，却只描述了其中一个，没有考虑不同浇水方式的影响。

To carry out a more useful comparison, we started again. We used matching containers, divided the seeds between them without selecting the strongest ones, and recorded the same watering routine. Both groups received the usual room light, while one received additional light from the lamp. We also marked the measuring point so that two people would not choose different starting positions.

> 为了作更有用的比较，我们重新开始。使用相同容器，不挑选最健壮的种子来分组，并记录统一的浇水安排。两组都接受通常的室内光，其中一组额外接受台灯照明。我们还标明测量起点，避免两个人选择不同位置。

The second result was less satisfying to look at. Some plants under the lamp were taller; others were shorter than plants in the comparison group. We entered every measurement, including the small plants we might once have ignored. There was no clear pattern strong enough for us to repeat our first claim with confidence. A tidier story would have required leaving out inconvenient observations.

> 第二次的结果看起来没那么令人满意。灯下有些苗更高，有些却比对照组更矮。我们记录每一次测量，包括过去可能被忽略的小苗。没有出现足够清晰的规律，让我们有把握重复最初的主张。若想讲一个更整齐的故事，就得删掉那些不方便的观察。

That did not establish that extra light could never matter. Our small test involved one kind of plant, one lamp and a short period. We had not measured all the relevant conditions, so we could not rule out every alternative explanation. Even our choice of height as the main outcome deserved another question: taller did not automatically mean healthier or stronger.

> 这并不能证明额外光照永远没有作用。我们的小测试只涉及一种植物、一盏灯和一段较短时间，也没有测量所有相关条件，因此无法排除每种替代解释。甚至把高度作为主要结果的做法也值得追问：更高并不自动意味着更健康或更强壮。

On the basis of the second test, we rewrote the conclusion rather than adjusting the measurements to rescue the prediction. We described what we had done, what varied and what remained uncertain. The result did not give us the confident answer we had wanted. It did give the next group a clearer starting point, because they could see which question had actually been tested.

> 根据第二次测试，我们修改了结论，而不是调整测量值来挽救预测。我们写清做了什么、哪些地方有差异，以及什么仍不确定。结果没有给出我们期待的肯定答案，却让下一组有更清楚的起点，因为他们能看出究竟检验了什么问题。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| carry out | 进行；实施 | carry out a test / an experiment。 |
| account for | 解释；考虑进去 | 此处指考虑可能影响比较的因素。 |
| rule out | 排除 | 未检测到不等于完全排除。 |
| on the basis of | 根据 | 说明结论所依赖的证据。 |

#### 理解检查

1. What weakened the team’s first comparison?
   - A. The two sets of plants differed in more than light.
   - B. They had no written prediction.
   - C. Every plant died.

2. What did the second test establish?
   - A. The lamp always improves growth.
   - B. The team could not confidently support its original claim under those conditions.
   - C. Light has no effect on any plant.

**参考答案**：1. A — 浇水方式和位置等条件也不同，不能把差异全归因于光照。；2. B — 结果只限制在此次条件下，不能扩展为普遍结论。

#### 复述
按“预测—第一次问题—修改—观察—结论边界”复述实验，指出一个尚未排除的解释。

#### 换条件表达
解释某次测试结果不明显，因此需要重复测试，而不是宣布两个方案完全相同。

参考表达（不唯一）：We did not observe a clear difference in this test. We need more evidence before saying the two options perform the same way.

---

## 精编 34：Three Objects from the Same Street
### 同一条街的三件旧物

**历史与文化 · 观察叙事 · 进阶 · 约 379 词**

**学习目标**：比较不同来源的历史材料，说明可确认信息与解释之间的距离。

原主题对应历史原稿 14；本篇为独立重写稿。

The exhibition occupied a single room above a library. Its title was the name of a street I passed every day. Instead of presenting a complete history, the curator had placed three objects on separate tables: a photograph, a shop receipt and a recorded interview. All three concerned the same short stretch of pavement, but none could tell its story alone.

> 展览位于图书馆楼上的一个房间，标题就是我每天经过的街名。策展人没有展示一套完整历史，而是在三张桌上分别放了一张照片、一张商店票据和一段访谈录音。三件材料都与同一小段街道有关，却没有任何一件能独自讲完它的故事。

The photograph appeared to date back to the early part of the twentieth century. People stood outside a bakery, facing the camera. I first noticed their formal clothes and imagined a special celebration. The label offered a more cautious description: the exact occasion was unknown. A posed image could reveal buildings and clothing while leaving out the ordinary movement that had stopped for the photographer.

> 照片似乎来自二十世纪初。人们站在面包店外，面向相机。我先注意到正式的衣着，猜想那是某种庆典。标签的描述更谨慎：具体场合不详。摆拍能显示建筑和服装，却会遗漏为拍照而暂停的日常活动。

The receipt listed flour, fuel and delivery charges. Its numbers made the shop feel less like a charming scene and more like a business with bills to pay. Yet the figures did not tell us how much the owner earned or how workers were treated. Several prices had been crossed out and replaced, and the curator had preserved both versions rather than quietly choosing one.

> 票据列着面粉、燃料和配送费用。这些数字让商店不再只是迷人的画面，也是一家需要支付账单的生意。但数字无法告诉我们店主收入多少、雇员受到怎样的对待。几项价格被划掉重写，策展人保留了两个版本，没有悄悄选定一个。

In the interview, a former resident remembered buying bread there as a child. Her description included a step at the entrance that her grandmother found difficult. That detail was invisible in the photograph until I looked again. Her account gave meaning to a feature I had taken for granted. It also depended on a memory recorded many years after the visits she described.

> 访谈里，一位以前的居民记得童年在那里买面包。她提到入口有一级台阶，祖母走起来很吃力。直到重新看照片，我才注意到这个细节。她的讲述赋予了一个我习以为常的结构以意义，但那段记忆也是在多年后才被录下的。

The display asked visitors to piece together a description using all three sources. I could say that there had been a bakery and identify some of its costs. I could also report one person's remembered experience of entering it. I could not honestly turn those fragments into a confident claim that everyone on the street had lived in the same way.

> 展览请观众综合三个来源拼出一段描述。我可以说这里曾有一家面包店，并指出它的部分成本，也能转述一个人记得的进店经历。但我不能诚实地把这些碎片，变成“街上所有人都过着同一种生活”的肯定主张。

Outside, the bakery building had become a phone shop. I noticed the entrance step before I noticed the sign. The exhibition had not replaced my present-day street with a complete picture of the past. It had changed the questions I asked of both: whose experience was visible, which details had survived, and what would be missing from the record we left behind?

> 走出展览后，原面包店已经成了手机店。我先看见入口台阶，才注意招牌。展览并没有用一幅完整的过去图景替代眼前街道，却改变了我对过去和现在的提问：谁的经历被看见，什么细节保存下来，而我们留下的记录又会漏掉什么？

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| date back to | 可追溯到 | 主语通常是物品、传统或建筑。 |
| take for granted（原文：taken for granted） | 认为理所当然 | 过去习惯未必与现在相同。 |
| leave out（原文：leaving out） | 遗漏；省略 | leave / left / left。 |
| piece together | 拼合线索 | 由零散材料重建有限的理解。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did the photograph not settle who used the street?
   - A. It showed only one moment from one viewpoint.
   - B. It had no buildings.
   - C. It was printed in colour.

2. What did the exhibition invite visitors to compare?
   - A. Three accounts that provided different kinds of evidence.
   - B. Three identical copies of one object.
   - C. Only the price of old objects.

**参考答案**：1. A — 照片只保存了某个时刻和视角，并不代表街上所有人的生活。；2. A — 照片、票据和口述记忆提供的信息类型不同。

#### 复述
分别说明照片、票据、口述记忆能提供什么，又各自遗漏了什么。

#### 换条件表达
描述一张家庭旧照时，区分照片所见与后来亲人讲述的故事。

参考表达（不唯一）：The photograph shows four people outside a shop. According to my aunt, it was taken on the day the shop opened, but that detail is not written on the photograph.

---

## 精编 35：Before the Appointment
### 就诊之前，我准备了什么

**健康生活 · 叙事 · 中级 · 约 335 词**

**学习目标**：清楚描述时间线、实际体验和想向专业人员核实的问题。

原主题对应历史原稿 15；本篇为独立重写稿。

When I booked the appointment, the receptionist asked what I wanted to discuss. I gave a short answer, then realised that I had described the last bad afternoon rather than the whole week. The events were clear in my memory as separate moments, but I had not put them in order. I decided to prepare before I went.

> 预约时，前台问我想咨询什么。我简短回答后才意识到，自己描述的是最近一个不舒服的下午，而不是整周的情况。这些事件作为片段很清楚，我却没按时间排列。于是，我决定就诊前先整理一下。

I began to write down when the discomfort had started, how long each episode had lasted and what I had actually noticed. I avoided filling the page with guesses about the cause. Where I was unsure of a time, I wrote "approximately" instead of pretending to remember exactly. That helped me separate observation from explanation.

> 我开始记下不适何时出现、每次持续多久，以及自己实际注意到什么。我没有写满关于病因的猜测。记不准的时间，就注明“大约”，而不是假装记得很精确。这样能把观察与解释分开。

I also listed the medicines and other products I was taking, using their labels rather than relying on names I half remembered. I brought the list with me and left room for questions. The receptionist had explained the clinic's arrival arrangements when I booked, so I checked those details again before leaving home.

> 我也列出正在使用的药物和其他产品，名称以包装标签为准，没有依赖模糊记忆。我带上清单，并留出写问题的位置。预约时，前台已解释到达后的安排，我出门前又核对了一次。

During the appointment, I initially tried to read every note aloud. The clinician asked me to start with the concern that mattered most to me. Once I had explained that, we used the timeline to fill in the detail. Preparing had helped, but it did not mean the conversation had to follow my page from top to bottom.

> 就诊时，我起初试图把每条笔记都念出来。医生请我先说最在意的问题。说明后，我们再用时间线补充细节。提前准备有帮助，但不意味着谈话必须从上到下照着纸上的顺序进行。

Near the end, I worried that I might leave something out. We went back to my questions and checked which had been answered. I asked the clinician to go over the next steps in plain language and to explain when I should contact the clinic again. If I had not understood something, I said so rather than nodding.

> 快结束时，我担心遗漏问题。我们回到清单，确认哪些已经得到回答。我请医生用通俗语言复核下一步，并说明何时需要再次联系诊所。没听懂的地方，我会直接说出来，而不是只点头。

At home, I added the agreed arrangements to my calendar and kept the notes together. The list had not diagnosed anything, and someone else's appointment could require a different conversation. Its value was simpler: it helped me give a clearer account and check that I understood what had actually been agreed.

> 回家后，我把已经商定的安排记进日历，保留好相关笔记。清单没有作出诊断，别人的就诊也可能需要完全不同的谈话。它的价值更简单：帮助我把经历讲清楚，并确认自己理解了实际商定的内容。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| write down | 记下 | write / wrote / written。 |
| leave something out | 遗漏某事 | 避免在叙述中漏掉重要信息。 |
| in order | 按顺序 | 此处表示按时间整理。 |
| go over | 逐一查看；复核 | go over a list together。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did the narrator make a list?
   - A. To decide on a treatment independently.
   - B. To describe events without forgetting details.
   - C. To replace the appointment.

2. What did the narrator do before leaving?
   - A. Asked the clinician to clarify the agreed next steps.
   - B. Guessed an unclear instruction.
   - C. Copied another patient’s plan.

**参考答案**：1. B — 清单用于准确叙述，并没有替代专业判断。；2. A — 离开前逐项复核下一步及需要再次联系的情形。

#### 复述
复述从预约、整理信息到确认下一步的过程，区分观察和自己的猜测。

#### 换条件表达
向医生说明症状从周一开始，但你不确定是否与工作安排有关。

参考表达（不唯一）：It started on Monday. I noticed it after work, but I’m not sure whether the two are connected.

---

## 精编 36：Before I Clicked Accept
### 点下接受之前

**工作协作 · 决策叙事 · 进阶 · 约 370 词**

**学习目标**：阅读一段示例条款，明确费用基准、通知期限与尚待解释的部分。

原主题对应历史原稿 16；本篇为独立重写稿。

The shared office looked exactly right: close to the station, quiet enough for calls and available two days a week. The booking page displayed a single monthly price and an inviting button marked "Accept." I was ready to click when I noticed a link to the terms. I opened it, expecting little more than the same information in longer sentences.

> 这间共享办公室看起来完全合适：靠近车站，安静到可以打电话，每周能用两天。预订页面显示一个月费和醒目的“接受”按钮。我正要点击，注意到条款链接。打开时，我以为里面只是把相同信息写得更长。

The first question concerned what the price covered. One paragraph referred to "standard facilities," while another said customers could be responsible for certain equipment charges. I could not tell whether using a screen during a meeting would cost extra. That uncertainty mattered more to my budget than the welcoming photograph of the room.

> 第一个问题是价格包含什么。一段提到“标准设施”，另一段又说某些设备费用可能由客户承担。我看不出开会用显示屏是否要另付费。相比房间的温馨照片，这个不确定之处对我的预算更重要。

Next, I looked at how the arrangement would come to an end. The example agreement required notice before a stated renewal date, but the page did not make that date obvious. I had nearly assumed that stopping my visits would stop the payments. Nothing I had read supported that assumption. I wrote down the exact wording instead of trying to remember its general meaning.

> 接着，我看了安排如何结束。这份示例协议要求在指定续约日前通知，但页面没有清楚显示那个日期。我差点以为停止到访就会停止收费，然而已读内容并不支持这个假设。我记下原句，没有只凭大概印象。

I emailed the provider with three numbered questions: did the displayed amount refer to one calendar month, which equipment was included, and what date would the notice period be counted from? I asked for the answers in writing. The reply explained the screen charge and attached a schedule showing the renewal date. It also corrected a sentence the provider agreed was ambiguous.

> 我发邮件提出三个编号问题：显示金额是否对应一个自然月，包含哪些设备，通知期从哪天开始算。我要求书面答复。回复解释了显示屏费用，并附上写有续约日期的时间表。服务方也修改了一句他们认可存在歧义的文字。

The answers did not make the offer automatically good or bad. They made it possible to compare it with another room whose advertised price was higher but included the equipment I needed. I calculated the likely cost for my own pattern of use. A lower headline price was not the same as a lower total under the conditions that applied to me.

> 这些答案没有自动证明报价好或坏，但让我能与另一间办公室比较。后者广告价格更高，却包含我需要的设备。我按自己的使用方式估算成本。在适用于我的条件下，标示价格更低，不等于总花费更低。

I eventually chose the other room and saved both offers with the correspondence. This fictional booking does not establish a rule about anyone else's agreement or rights. Its lesson was a reading task: identify the commitment, locate the conditions and ask about the wording you cannot confidently explain. I wanted my decision to rest on the offer I was accepting, not on a convenient version I had imagined.

> 最后我选了另一间，并把两份报价和往来邮件一起保存。这个虚构预订情境不构成关于其他协议或权利的规则。它呈现的是一项阅读任务：找出承诺、定位条件，并追问自己无法准确解释的措辞。我希望决定依据的是实际接受的报价，而不是自己想象中更方便的版本。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| be responsible for | 负责 | 介词 for 后接名词/doing。 |
| in writing | 以书面形式 | 用于保留明确答复。 |
| refer to | 指的是；提及 | 需核对指代对象。 |
| come to an end | 结束 | 描述期限的终点。 |

#### 理解检查

1. What was unclear about the quoted price?
   - A. Whether it was monthly and whether equipment was included.
   - B. Whether the room had a window.
   - C. Whether the provider had an email address.

2. What did the narrator do with the unclear cancellation wording?
   - A. Asked for clarification and kept the reply.
   - B. Assumed it meant immediate cancellation.
   - C. Treated this example as a rule for every agreement.

**参考答案**：1. A — 需确认月费口径及设备是否另计。；2. A — 在作决定前要求解释，并保存书面答复。

#### 复述
复述三个核对点，说明哪些已得到回答，哪些仍取决于具体协议。

#### 换条件表达
就一份报价追问：三十天通知期是从发送邮件当天开始计算吗？

参考表达（不唯一）：Could you confirm in writing whether the thirty-day notice period starts on the day I send the email?

---

## 精编 37：Thirteen, Not Thirty
### 是十三，不是三十

**学习成长 · 叙事 · 中级 · 约 357 词**

**学习目标**：描述一次误听的形成和纠正过程，练习数字澄清与事实核对。

原主题对应历史原稿 17；本篇为独立重写稿。

I was arranging chairs for a workshop when another volunteer asked why I had put out so many. "Thirty people," I said. I was certain of the number because I had written it at the top of my notebook in large, careful letters. She looked surprised. Her message from the organiser said thirteen, and the room suddenly seemed much larger than we needed.

> 我正在为工作坊摆椅子，另一位志愿者问为什么摆这么多。“三十个人。”我很确定，因为笔记本最上方用又大又工整的字写着这个数字。她却很惊讶：组织者给她的信息是十三人。房间忽然显得大得超出需要。

The previous evening, I had taken a brief call while walking home. Traffic was loud, and I expected the group to be about the same size as the month before. When I heard a number ending in a similar sound, I wrote thirty without asking a follow-up question. My expectation had supplied a comfortable answer to an unclear moment.

> 前一天晚上，我在回家路上接了个简短电话。车流声很大，我又预期人数与上个月差不多。听到一个尾音相近的数字时，便写下“三十”，没有继续问。原本模糊的一刻，被符合预期的答案填补了。

By the next morning, I could remember the note more clearly than the call. I even felt I could hear the organiser saying the larger number. That feeling did not tell us which record was correct. We stopped moving furniture and opened the original email together. It said thirteen, with a list of the participants underneath.

> 到了第二天早上，我对笔记的记忆，比对电话本身还清楚，甚至觉得能听见组织者说出较大的数字。但这种感觉无法说明哪份记录正确。我们停下搬家具，一起打开原始邮件，上面写着十三人，下面还附了名单。

There was no need to jump to a conclusion about anyone being careless in every part of their life. I had made one specific mistake under specific conditions. The useful questions were practical: which information mattered enough to confirm, and how could we make the confirmation easy? We added a short booking summary to the shared document.

> 没必要因此就断言某个人在生活各方面都粗心。我是在特定条件下犯了一个具体错误。有用的问题是实际的：哪些信息重要到需要确认，怎样让确认更容易？我们在共享文档里增加了一份简短预订摘要。

Now, when I take a booking call, I read back the date, time and number of people before ending it. If a number is hard to hear, I ask the speaker to say its digits separately. I also request a written message for the details that affect space or payment. The extra question can feel awkward, but moving seventeen unnecessary chairs felt more awkward.

> 现在接预订电话，我会在挂断前复述日期、时间和人数。数字难听清时，请对方逐位说。涉及场地或付款的细节，我也会要求文字确认。多问一句可能有点尴尬，但搬走十七把多余椅子更尴尬。

The next workshop did have thirty participants. This time, I did not accept the number simply because it matched the previous error. I checked the updated list, counted the seats and asked another volunteer to double-check the layout. Confidence was useful for getting started; the record was what allowed us to finish preparing the right room.

> 下一次工作坊确实有三十人。这次，我没有因为数字与上次错误相同就直接接受。我检查更新后的名单，数座位，并请另一位志愿者复核布局。有信心能帮助开始，但记录才让我们最终准备好合适的房间。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| be certain of（原文：was certain of） | 确信 | 确信的感觉不等于已核实。 |
| double-check | 再次核对 | 用于数字、日期和关键信息。 |
| jump to a conclusion | 过早下结论 | 在信息不足时形成判断。 |
| read back | 复述核对 | read back a number / an address。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did the narrator initially trust the number thirty?
   - A. It fitted what they expected and was written neatly in their notes.
   - B. Three people had independently confirmed it.
   - C. The organiser showed a printed list.

2. What finally resolved the disagreement?
   - A. The original email from the organiser.
   - B. A vote among the volunteers.
   - C. Repeating the same memory more loudly.

**参考答案**：1. A — 数字符合预期，清晰笔记又增加了确信感，但没有独立确认。；2. A — 原始邮件明确写着十三人。

#### 复述
按“听到什么—以为什么—拿什么核对—怎样改流程”复述，不把记忆问题说成普遍心理定律。

#### 换条件表达
在电话里确认六十还是十六，并请求书面名单。

参考表达（不唯一）：Did you say sixty or sixteen? Could you send me the list so I can double-check the number?

---

## 精编 38：A Busy Shop with an Empty Account
### 生意忙，账户却没钱

**工作协作 · 案例叙事 · 进阶 · 约 364 词**

**学习目标**：通过虚构数字理解销售、成本和到账时间的不同，核对比较口径。

原主题对应历史原稿 18；本篇为独立重写稿。

When Daniel's small print shop received its largest order of the year, he invited the team for lunch. The customer wanted five hundred notebooks at twenty dollars each. Ten thousand dollars sounded impressive beside an ordinary week's sales. Two days later, however, Daniel was staring at the bank account, wondering how to pay for the paper needed to begin the job.

> 丹尼尔的小印刷店接到当年最大订单时，他请团队吃了午饭。客户要五百本笔记本，每本二十美元。一万美元与普通一周的销售相比很可观。但两天后，他盯着银行账户，盘算如何先付制作所需的纸张款。

The order was real, but most of the money would not come in until after delivery. The paper supplier required payment in advance. Staff still needed to be paid, and the shop's rent was due before the customer expected to receive the notebooks. A busy production schedule did not move the customer's payment date forward.

> 订单是真的，但大部分钱要交货后才到账。纸张供应商要求预付，员工工资仍要支付，店租也在客户收货前到期。生产排得再忙，也不会让客户的付款日自动提前。

Daniel asked his colleague Nina to break down the order. In this fictional example, paper and covers would cost three thousand dollars, outside printing another two thousand, and packaging five hundred. The difference between those listed costs and the order value was not automatically money available to spend. Other shop expenses still existed, and some costs were estimates rather than final invoices.

> 丹尼尔请同事妮娜拆分订单。在这个虚构例子中，纸张和封面花三千美元，外包印刷两千，包装五百。订单金额减去这些列出的成本，并不自动等于可随意支出的钱。店里还有其他开支，而且部分数字只是估算，不是最终账单。

They made a second table arranged by date rather than customer. It showed money currently available, payments already committed and expected receipts. That view exposed a gap in the middle of the month. Even if the customer paid exactly when agreed, the account could run short before the delivery. The problem was partly one of timing, not simply the size of the sale.

> 他们又做了一张按日期、而非按客户排列的表，显示现有资金、已承诺支出和预计收款。这个视图暴露了月中的缺口：即使客户完全按约付款，交货前账户仍可能不够。问题部分在时间安排，不只是销售额大小。

Daniel discussed the schedule with the customer and supplier before making further commitments. He also kept estimates separate from confirmed amounts and asked for professional help with the business's wider finances. A single table could not resolve every cost or contractual question. It could make the immediate uncertainty visible instead of hiding it behind a large total.

> 丹尼尔在作出更多承诺前，与客户和供应商商量时间安排。他把估算和已确认金额分开，也就整体财务向专业人员求助。一张表无法解决所有成本和合同问题，但能让眼前的不确定性显现，而不是藏在大总额后面。

The team still celebrated winning the work. They simply stopped using the order value as an answer to every financial question. At later reviews, Nina asked which period each figure covered, whether it had been paid and what remained to be spent. Their new habit was to set money aside for identified commitments before treating a promising month as spare cash.

> 团队仍为赢得业务庆祝，只是不再用订单总额回答所有财务问题。后来的复盘中，妮娜会问每个数字覆盖什么期间、是否收款、还有什么支出。他们开始为已确定的承诺预留资金，而不是把看起来不错的一个月直接理解为多余现金。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| come in | 进来；到账 | 这里指款项实际收到。 |
| payment in advance | 预付款 | in advance 表示提前；pay 是动词，payment 是名词。 |
| break down | 拆分 | break down a total into parts。 |
| set money aside | 预留资金 | 为特定用途留出金额。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why was the account short of cash despite large orders?
   - A. Some customers had not yet paid, while supplies had to be paid for first.
   - B. Every order had been cancelled.
   - C. The owner had forgotten to count the number of products.

2. What did the revised table separate?
   - A. Order value, expected costs and payment dates.
   - B. Only product colours.
   - C. Revenue by the owner’s favourite customers.

**参考答案**：1. A — 销售约定和实际到账不同步，而材料需要提前付款。；2. A — 拆分金额、成本与到账日后，才能看清资金时间差。

#### 复述
用文中的一个订单解释：金额看上去很大，为什么仍可能需要先付钱。

#### 换条件表达
核对一份业务报告：这二十万元是已收款，还是已确认但未收款的订单金额？

参考表达（不唯一）：Does the two hundred thousand yuan refer to money already received, or to confirmed orders that have not been paid yet?

---

## 精编 39：Before Sharing the Headline
### 转发标题之前

**科技与信息 · 调查叙事 · 中级 · 约 350 词**

**学习目标**：检查消息的时间、范围和来源，说明原始表述与转发标题的差别。

原主题对应历史原稿 19；本篇为独立重写稿。

A message appeared in our neighbourhood group just before I left for work: "Station closing tomorrow. All services affected." Several people immediately changed their plans. One offered to drive others into the city. I began to pass on the message to a colleague, then noticed that the attached image had been cropped so closely that no date or source remained.

> 出门上班前，邻里群里出现一条消息：“车站明天关闭，全部班次受影响。”几个人马上修改计划，有人提出开车带大家进城。我正要转发给同事，注意到附图裁得太紧，日期和来源都不见了。

The wording sounded definite, but I did not know what "station" referred to. There were two stations with similar names in the area. I searched for the transport operator's original notice and found a paragraph about repairs at the north entrance. The work affected access for a few hours, not every train for a whole day.

> 措辞很肯定，但我不知道“车站”究竟指哪里。附近有两个名称相似的站。我查找运营方的原始通知，找到一段关于北入口维修的说明。工程影响的是几个小时的出入，并不是整天所有列车。

The notice also carried a date from the previous month. A new message had made an old image look current. That did not prove the original author had intended to mislead anyone; the image might have been copied without its context. It did mean that the information was out of date for tomorrow's journey.

> 通知还标着上个月的日期。新发的消息让旧图片看起来像当前消息。这不能证明最初发布者有意误导，图片也可能只是脱离背景被复制。但对明天的出行而言，这份信息确实已经过时。

Before replying, I checked the current service page as well. I wanted to avoid replacing one unsupported claim with another. Then I posted the original link, explained the date and entrance restrictions, and said which part of tomorrow's timetable I had actually checked. I did not claim to know that every possible service would run normally.

> 回复前，我又查看了当前运营页面，避免用另一个缺乏依据的说法替代原消息。随后贴出原链接，解释日期和入口限制，并说明实际查过明天时刻表的哪一部分。我没有声称所有可能的班次都会正常运行。

The person who had shared the image thanked me and added a correction. One neighbour still needed help finding a step-free entrance, which was a different question from whether the station was closed. The group could now focus on that practical concern. A calmer headline would not have supplied all the answers either, but the missing details had mattered.

> 转发图片的人道谢并补充了更正。另一个邻居仍需要找到无台阶入口，这与“车站是否关闭”是不同问题。群里终于可以讨论这个实际需求。即使标题写得更平和，也不会自动给出全部答案，但缺失的细节确实很重要。

I still read quick updates on my phone. I simply pause when a claim would cause someone to cancel, pay, travel or worry. Before sharing, I look for the original wording, its date and the people or places it covers. Those checks do not make every message certain. They make it clearer what I know and what I am merely repeating.

> 我仍会用手机快速看消息，只是在一条说法可能让人取消安排、付款、出行或担忧时停一下。转发前，我寻找原文、日期，以及适用的人群或地点。这些核对不能让所有消息都变得确定，但能让我分清哪些是已知信息，哪些只是重复别人的话。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| out of date | 过时的 | 不同于 wrong；信息可能曾经适用。 |
| refer to（原文：referred to） | 指的是 | 确认文字究竟覆盖谁和哪里。 |
| cropped | 被裁切的 | crop a photograph 裁剪照片；裁掉的背景可能影响理解。 |
| pass on | 传递；转发 | pass on a message / pass it on。 |

#### 理解检查

1. What was misleading about the forwarded message?
   - A. It removed the date and location limits.
   - B. It used a smaller photograph.
   - C. It included too many source links.

2. What did the narrator send to the group?
   - A. The original notice with the relevant limits explained.
   - B. Only a more dramatic headline.
   - C. A claim that every forwarded message is false.

**参考答案**：1. A — 原本局部、短期的调整被标题写成普遍变化。；2. A — 补上来源、日期和地点，没有扩大结论。

#### 复述
先转述群消息的说法，再说明原始通知的实际范围，最后说你会怎样回复。

#### 换条件表达
解释通知只适用于周一上午的北入口，而非整栋楼全天关闭。

参考表达（不唯一）：The notice refers only to the north entrance on Monday morning. It does not say that the whole building will be closed all day.

---

## 精编 40：The Promise I Could Not Keep
### 我没能兑现的承诺

**思考与选择 · 反思叙事 · 进阶 · 约 389 词**

**学习目标**：讨论承诺、限制与责任的冲突，用具体后果支持观点。

原主题对应历史原稿 20；本篇为独立重写稿。

When my friend Ben asked whether I could help him move, I said yes before opening my calendar. I wanted to be the sort of friend he could count on. The move was two weeks away, which made the promise feel easy. I pictured a few boxes, a short drive and lunch afterwards. Ben, meanwhile, began planning around a whole morning of help.

> 朋友本问我能否帮忙搬家时，我没打开日历就答应了。我希望成为他可以依靠的朋友。搬家还在两周后，承诺显得很轻松。我想象的是几箱东西、一段短途开车，之后一起午饭；本却开始按“我能帮整个上午”来安排。

Three days before the move, I learned that I needed to accompany a family member to an appointment. The times overlapped. I could not reasonably be in both places, but I delayed telling Ben because I hoped some convenient solution would appear. That delay protected me from an uncomfortable conversation while reducing the time he had to find another arrangement.

> 搬家前三天，我得知需要陪家人去一个预约，时间重叠了。我不可能同时出现在两处，却拖着没告诉本，希望事情会出现方便的解决办法。拖延让我暂时避开尴尬谈话，却缩短了他另作安排的时间。

When I finally called, Ben was angry. He had turned down another offer of help and booked a van on the assumption that I would drive it. I explained the family situation, but that did not remove the consequences of my promise. Two things could be true together: my new responsibility mattered, and he had good reason to feel let down.

> 终于打电话时，本很生气。他已经拒绝另一位朋友的帮助，并在认定我会开车的前提下租了货车。我解释了家里的情况，但这并没有消除承诺带来的后果。两件事可以同时成立：新的家庭责任很重要，而他也确实有理由失望。

I asked what problem was hardest to solve at short notice. It was the driving, not the number of boxes. With his agreement, I contacted another friend who could drive and offered to pay the additional rental cost caused by changing the collection time. I also helped pack the evening before. These actions were useful, but they did not turn the missed promise into a kept one.

> 我问他，临时最难解决的是什么。是开车，不是箱子数量。经他同意，我联系另一位能开车的朋友，并提出承担因调整取车时间增加的租金。前一晚我也帮忙打包。这些行动有用，却不能把失约变成守约。

It took longer to make up for the loss of trust. For a while, Ben checked details with me more than he used to. I found that uncomfortable, then remembered who had made the uncertainty necessary. An apology could acknowledge the problem; only later behaviour could give him a reason to rely on me again. I could not demand an immediate return to the old confidence.

> 信任受损需要更久才能弥补。一段时间里，本比以前更常向我确认细节。我觉得不自在，随后想起，是谁让这种确认变得必要。道歉能承认问题，只有后续行为才能给他再次依靠我的理由。我不能要求信任立刻恢复。

Now, I try to make smaller, clearer commitments: an hour of packing, one return journey, a call by a certain time. If I cannot follow through, I tell the other person as soon as I know and ask which consequences need attention. Being helpful no longer means saying yes to the largest imagined version of a request. It means allowing someone to plan with an honest account of what I can provide.

> 现在，我尽量作更小、更清楚的承诺：帮忙打包一小时、接送一趟、在某个时间前回电话。如果无法落实，确定后就尽快告知，并询问哪些后果需要处理。乐于助人，不再意味着答应自己想象中最大版本的请求，而是让别人能依据我真实可提供的帮助作安排。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| count on | 指望；依赖 | count on someone to do something。 |
| make up for | 弥补 | 不一定能完全消除影响。 |
| at short notice | 在很短的提前通知下 | 强调留给对方准备的时间少。 |
| follow through | 把承诺落实到底 | 常与 on a promise 搭配。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why was simply apologising insufficient?
   - A. The friend still needed a way to move the furniture.
   - B. The friend had not heard the promise.
   - C. The move was cancelled by the building.

2. How did the narrator change future commitments?
   - A. Offered specific limits before agreeing.
   - B. Refused every request permanently.
   - C. Promised more quickly to look helpful.

**参考答案**：1. A — 道歉不能直接解决周六搬运的实际缺口。；2. A — 以后先说明自己能提供的具体时间和能力，再让对方决定。

#### 复述
说明冲突、两个人分别受到的影响、补救行动及其仍未解决的部分。

#### 换条件表达
你只能帮忙一小时，不能开车。向朋友清楚说明，让对方自行决定是否足够。

参考表达（不唯一）：I can help for an hour on Saturday morning, but I can’t drive. Would that still be useful, or do you need someone who can stay longer?

---

## 精编 41：Before the First Loaf Leaves the Oven
### 第一炉面包出炉之前

**人物与生活 · 访谈 · 中级 · 约 349 词**

**学习目标**：从较长问答中提取作息、数量决策与意外处理。

Interviewer: What time does your working day begin? Baker: Usually at four thirty. I check the dough prepared the previous evening before turning to the day's orders. By the time the first customer arrives at seven, several hours of work have already happened. Opening the door is the beginning of sales, not the beginning of making bread.

> 采访者：你通常几点开始工作？面包师：一般四点半。我先检查前一晚准备的面团，再看当天订单。第一位顾客七点到时，我们已经做了好几个小时。开门是开始卖面包，不是开始做面包。

Interviewer: How do you decide how much to make? Baker: Confirmed orders come first. Then I look at the day of the week, recent sales and anything unusual nearby. A school event can change the morning. I cannot predict every customer, so I allow for some variation rather than treating yesterday's number as a rule.

> 采访者：怎么决定做多少？面包师：先看已确认订单，再看星期几、近期销售和附近有没有特殊活动。一次学校活动就可能改变整个早晨。我无法预测每位顾客，所以会留出调整余量，不把昨天的数字当成规则。

Interviewer: Is selling everything always a good result? Baker: Not necessarily. If we run out of the most popular loaf at nine, we may disappoint people who regularly come at ten. But making too much creates another problem. I record when things sell, not just whether they sell. The time tells me something the final total cannot.

> 采访者：全部卖完总是好事吗？面包师：不一定。如果最受欢迎的面包九点就卖光，经常十点来的顾客会失望。但做太多也有问题。我会记什么时候卖出，而不只记有没有卖出。时间能告诉我总数看不出的东西。

Interviewer: What happens when a delivery is late? Baker: I check which ingredients we already have and simplify the range. I would rather offer three things at the promised time than promise six I cannot finish. The person at the counter needs the same information, or customers hear a different answer from whoever happens to be serving.

> 采访者：原料配送迟到怎么办？面包师：先看现有材料，缩减品类。我宁愿按承诺提供三样，也不答应做完六样却办不到。柜台同事也必须知道相同信息，否则顾客会因接待人不同而听到不同答案。

Interviewer: Can a new colleague learn by watching? Baker: Watching helps, but I also ask them to describe what they notice. "Bake until ready" is not useful to a beginner. We work on one stage at a time and compare the result with an example. I need to know whether my instructions were clear, not just whether someone can copy my movements.

> 采访者：新同事看你操作就能学会吗？面包师：观察有帮助，但我也会请他们描述注意到了什么。“烤到好了”对初学者没用。我们每次练一个阶段，把结果与示例比较。我需要知道指引是否清楚，不只是对方能否模仿动作。

Interviewer: What do you enjoy most? Baker: The moment a regular customer tells us what happened to the bread after it left the shop: a family breakfast, a picnic, someone visiting after a long journey. We spend hours thinking about texture and timing. Their stories remind me that the product eventually becomes part of somebody else's ordinary day.

> 采访者：你最享受什么？面包师：熟客告诉我们面包离店后的故事时：一次家庭早餐、一场野餐，或远道而来的探访。我们花很多时间考虑口感和时机。他们的故事提醒我，产品最终会成为别人普通生活的一部分。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| by the time | 到……的时候 | 连接两个事件的先后。 |
| run out of | 用完；卖完 | run / ran / run。 |
| allow for | 为……留余量 | allow for delays / changes。 |
| at a time | 每次 | 不同于 at the same time。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why does the baker not make the same number of loaves every day?
   - A. Orders and the day’s expected demand differ.
   - B. The oven holds only one loaf.
   - C. Customers choose the number after closing.

2. What does the baker do when a delivery is late?
   - A. Adjusts the range and tells waiting customers.
   - B. Promises every product at the usual time anyway.
   - C. Stops taking notes.

**参考答案**：1. A — 订单和需求随星期、天气、活动等变化。；2. A — 先调整可提供品类，并说明变化，不作无法兑现的保证。

#### 复述
用第三人称介绍面包师的一天，包含开工、数量判断、交接和延误应对。

#### 换条件表达
以店员身份说明某种面包还需二十分钟，但另一种已经有货。

参考表达（不唯一）：The sourdough will be ready in about twenty minutes. We have the wholemeal loaves available now if you’d prefer not to wait.

---

## 精编 42：The Quietest Job in a Noisy Library
### 热闹图书馆里的安静工作

**人物与生活 · 访谈 · 中级 · 约 353 词**

**学习目标**：通过访谈理解同一空间中不同使用者的需求与取舍。

Interviewer: People imagine a library as a silent room. Is yours like that? Librarian: Parts of it are. Other parts contain children's activities, language groups and people asking how to use a printer. Silence is one service we provide, but it is not the only reason people come. A building can be busy without every corner being noisy.

> 采访者：人们常把图书馆想成安静的房间。你们这里也是吗？馆员：有些区域是。其他地方有儿童活动、语言小组，也有人来问打印机怎么用。安静是我们提供的一种服务，但不是大家来的唯一原因。整栋楼很忙，不代表每个角落都吵。

Interviewer: What makes a request difficult? Librarian: Someone may say they want a book about climate, history or computers. Those are enormous subjects. I ask what they want to do with the information. A school presentation, a personal decision and specialist research require different starting points. A good question often saves a long search through the wrong shelf.

> 采访者：什么请求比较难处理？馆员：有人说想找气候、历史或电脑方面的书。这些题目都很大。我会问，他想用信息做什么。课堂报告、个人决定和专业研究，需要不同的起点。问对问题，常能省下在错误书架前寻找的时间。

Interviewer: Do you find every answer for the reader? Librarian: I try to point them towards useful sources and show how to check what a source covers. Sometimes we look for an answer together. I also want them to continue on their own. Handing over a title is different from helping someone understand why that title might be useful.

> 采访者：每个答案都由你替读者找出来吗？馆员：我会引导他们找到有用来源，并演示怎样检查来源覆盖什么。有时一起找答案，但我也希望他们之后能独立继续。交给一个书名，与帮助理解它为什么有用，是两回事。

Interviewer: How do you manage groups and quiet study? Librarian: We make room for both in different areas and explain the arrangement before a problem grows. A group table near the entrance is not a promise that every conversation will be quiet. The study room upstairs has clearer restrictions. People need to know which space matches their task.

> 采访者：怎样兼顾小组和安静自习？馆员：通过不同区域给两者留空间，并在问题扩大前解释安排。入口附近的小组桌，并不保证每场谈话都很安静。楼上自习室的限制更明确。大家需要知道什么空间适合自己的任务。

Interviewer: Who finds the building hardest to use? Librarian: Sometimes the person who has never entered a library before. They may not know whether they can sit down without borrowing anything. We added a simple welcome sign and moved the help desk closer to the entrance. We learned more by watching someone arrive than by adding another page to the website.

> 采访者：谁觉得这栋楼最难使用？馆员：有时是从没进过图书馆的人。他们可能不知道，不借东西能不能坐下。我们加了简单欢迎牌，把咨询台移近入口。看一个人实际走进来，比再往网站加一页更能发现问题。

Interviewer: What counts as a successful day? Librarian: A reader may leave without a book but with the next step they needed. Last week, someone arrived looking for a particular manual. We found a newer guide and helped them locate a workshop. They left with a date and a contact, which mattered more than borrowing the original title.

> 采访者：怎样算成功的一天？馆员：读者可能没借书，却得到了所需的下一步。上周有人来找特定手册。我们找到更新的指南，又帮他找到工作坊。他离开时有日期和联系人，这比借到最初那本书更重要。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| look for | 寻找 | 不等于已找到的 find。 |
| point them towards | 引导他们找到方向 | point someone towards a source 引导查找来源。 |
| make room for | 为……留空间 | 这里涉及不同活动共存。 |
| on their own | 独自；独立 | their own 指本人自己。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why does the librarian ask about the reader’s purpose?
   - A. The purpose helps identify a useful starting point.
   - B. Every reader must pass a test.
   - C. The library only has one subject.

2. How does the library handle quiet and group work?
   - A. Creates different areas and explains their use.
   - B. Assumes everyone wants complete silence.
   - C. Removes every table.

**参考答案**：1. A — 同一个宽泛主题下，不同任务需要不同材料。；2. A — 通过分区和清晰说明，容纳不同使用需求。

#### 复述
介绍图书馆员如何处理找书、安静空间和首次来访者三个问题。

#### 换条件表达
你要为五分钟报告找材料，向馆员说明需要入门概览而不是专业论文。

参考表达（不唯一）：I’m preparing a five-minute presentation. Could you help me find a short introduction rather than a technical research paper?

---

## 精编 43：Changing Careers without a Perfect Plan
### 没有完美计划的转行

**工作协作 · 访谈 · 进阶 · 约 368 词**

**学习目标**：理解较长回答中的经历、证据与判断，区分希望和已验证能力。

Interviewer: Did you decide on a new career before leaving your old role? Sara: Not completely. I knew I wanted more analytical work, but that description covered many jobs. I had coordinated projects for six years and enjoyed finding patterns in delays. Enjoying that part of my job did not mean I already understood the daily work of an analyst.

> 采访者：离开原岗位前，你就确定新职业了吗？萨拉：没有完全确定。我知道想做更多分析工作，但这个描述包含很多职位。我做了六年项目协调，喜欢从延误中找规律。但喜欢原工作中的这一部分，不等于了解分析师每天实际做什么。

Interviewer: What did you do first? Sara: I spoke with three people whose jobs sounded interesting. Instead of asking whether they liked the profession, I asked what they had done the previous Tuesday. Their answers included cleaning inconsistent data, clarifying requests and explaining limitations. That helped me compare the actual tasks with the attractive picture I had formed around the title.

> 采访者：你先做了什么？萨拉：我找三个工作听起来有趣的人聊。没只问喜不喜欢这个职业，而是问上周二具体做了什么。回答包括整理不一致数据、澄清请求、解释局限。这样，我就能比较真实任务与自己围绕职位名称形成的美好印象。

Interviewer: How did you test your interest? Sara: I asked a community group for a small, non-sensitive project and used sample information they were comfortable sharing. The question was why event registration and attendance differed. I documented every assumption and produced a short explanation. It was modest work, but someone else had to understand the result and decide whether it was useful.
