> 采访者：怎样检验兴趣？萨拉：我向一个社区小组申请了非敏感的小项目，使用他们同意分享的示例信息，研究活动报名和实际出席为什么不同。我记录每个假设，写出简短解释。工作很小，但结果需要让别人理解，并判断有没有用。

Interviewer: What did it reveal? Sara: I could build on my experience asking operational questions. I was slower with the software than I expected, and my first chart made a small difference look dramatic. A reviewer pointed that out. Correcting the chart was more valuable evidence of learning than merely saying I was comfortable with numbers.

> 采访者：它揭示了什么？萨拉：提出运营问题的原有经验可以继续发挥作用。但软件操作比预期慢，第一张图还把小差异画得很夸张。审阅者指出了这一点。改好图，比只说自己擅长数字，更能证明我在学习。

Interviewer: Did that make the decision obvious? Sara: No. I had to consider income, study time and the kind of supervision I would need. I did not want to take on a role that required immediate independence in skills I had barely practised. I used the project to find out what to learn next and to rule out vacancies whose expectations were too far ahead.

> 采访者：于是决定就很明显了吗？萨拉：没有。我还得考虑收入、学习时间和需要什么指导。我不想承担一个要求立刻独立使用技能、而我几乎没练过的岗位。我用项目弄清下一步该学什么，也排除要求超前太多的职位。

Interviewer: How do you describe the move now? Sara: As a series of smaller choices rather than one brave leap. I changed direction after I had something concrete to show and a clearer account of the gaps. The plan was never perfect. It became more realistic each time another person questioned my work or I tried to do the job behind the description.

> 采访者：现在如何描述这次转行？萨拉：是一连串较小选择，而不是一次勇敢飞跃。有具体成果可展示、更清楚能力缺口后，我才改变方向。计划从来不完美，但每当别人质疑我的工作，或我真正尝试职位描述背后的任务时，它就更现实一点。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| build on | 在……基础上发展 | 已有经验如何用于新任务。 |
| find out | 弄清 | 通过行动获得具体信息。 |
| take on | 承担 | take on a project / responsibility。 |
| rule out | 排除 | 此处指检验后放弃某种选择。 |

#### 理解检查

1. What did Sara learn from the small project?
   - A. Which parts of the work she could demonstrate and which needed practice.
   - B. That every new job would be easy.
   - C. That her earlier experience was worthless.

2. Why did she speak with people doing the job?
   - A. To check the everyday work behind the attractive job title.
   - B. To ask them to guarantee a salary.
   - C. To avoid practising anything herself.

**参考答案**：1. A — 小项目提供可见成果，也暴露技能缺口。；2. A — 重点是了解日常职责，而不是只看职位名称。

#### 复述
按转行前的期待、现实接触、试做、发现的缺口和最终选择复述。

#### 换条件表达
向招聘者解释你可以带来的原有经验，同时坦诚一项仍在学习的技能。

参考表达（不唯一）：I can build on my experience coordinating projects, but I’m still learning the analysis tools. This small project shows what I can do so far.

---

## 精编 44：Learning a Language in an Ordinary Week
### 在普通的一周里学语言

**学习成长 · 访谈 · 中级 · 约 356 词**

**学习目标**：从学习者经历中识别输入、回忆与迁移，不把个人经验当作效果承诺。

Interviewer: What did your study routine look like at first? Mina: I collected interesting clips and listened to something new each evening. I highlighted words and felt busy. But when a colleague asked a simple question about my weekend, I struggled to come up with an answer. Recognising a phrase in a recording was different from finding it when I needed it.

> 采访者：最初怎样学习？米娜：我收集有趣片段，每晚听新内容，标出生词，感觉很充实。但同事简单问周末安排时，我却想不出怎么回答。听录音时认出一句话，与需要时能调出来，是不同的事。

Interviewer: What changed? Mina: I chose one short article for several days. First I listened without reading and wrote what I could hear. Then I checked the text and looked up expressions that blocked the meaning. I did not look up every unfamiliar word immediately. I wanted to understand the situation before spending the whole session inside a dictionary.

> 采访者：后来改了什么？米娜：同一篇短文练几天。先不看文本听，尽量写下听见的内容；再核对原文，查影响理解的表达。我没有一开始就查每个陌生词，希望先理解情境，不把整次学习都花在词典里。

Interviewer: When do you speak? Mina: After I understand a paragraph, I listen again and follow its rhythm. I pause where I need to. Repetition helps me get used to the sequence of words, but I do not assume that speaking along proves I can create the sentence myself. Later, I close the text and explain the paragraph in simpler language.

> 采访者：什么时候开口？米娜：理解一段后，再听并跟着节奏读，需要时暂停。重复让我逐渐熟悉词语顺序，但我不认为能跟着说就证明自己能造句。之后关上文本，用更简单的话解释那一段。

Interviewer: How do you make it less dependent on memory? Mina: I change one condition. If the article describes a meeting on Tuesday, I arrange it for Friday. If someone buys three tickets, I ask for two. The change can be small, but it forces me to choose a different word or form instead of reciting an unbroken sequence.

> 采访者：怎样减少只靠背诵？米娜：改一个条件。文章约周二开会，我改成周五；别人买三张票，我改买两张。变化可以很小，却会迫使我选择不同词语或词形，而不只是连续背出一串。

Interviewer: What happens on a busy day? Mina: I review one paragraph without looking before I add anything new. Sometimes that is all I do. On a quieter evening, I record an explanation and compare it with my earlier attempt. I note where I needed help. I do not give myself a perfect score just because I finished playing the audio.

> 采访者：忙的时候怎么办？米娜：先不看原文复习一段，再决定加不加新内容。有时就只做这一件事。空一点的晚上，我录下解释，与之前的尝试比较，并记下哪里需要帮助。我不会因为播完音频就给自己满分。

Interviewer: What improvement can you honestly claim? Mina: I can now arrange some everyday appointments with less hesitation, and I can ask for clarification when I miss a number. I still need practice outside those situations. This is my experience, not a promise that the same timetable will produce the same result for everyone.

> 采访者：你能诚实地确认什么进步？米娜：现在约一些日常事项时，我没那么犹豫了；漏听数字，也会主动确认。但其他情境仍需要练。这是我的经历，不代表所有人用同样时间表都会得到同样结果。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| get used to | 逐渐习惯 | get used to + 名词/doing。 |
| look up | 查阅 | look a word up / look it up。 |
| come up with | 想出 | come up with an answer。 |
| without looking | 不看资料 | 用于描述回忆条件。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did Mina stop changing material every day?
   - A. She recognised new material but could not reuse the expressions.
   - B. She had finished every book.
   - C. She disliked all audio.

2. How did she check whether she could use a phrase?
   - A. Changed the details and answered without looking.
   - B. Played the recording once.
   - C. Counted how many words she highlighted.

**参考答案**：1. A — 接触很多材料没有自动转化为可用表达。；2. A — 换时间、数字等条件，在不看原文时重新作答。

#### 复述
描述她的一周安排，明确同篇重复和换条件练习分别解决什么问题。

#### 换条件表达
用英文说明你已能跟读，但换一个日期就不会说了，因此要练迁移。

参考表达（不唯一）：I can repeat the passage, but I get stuck when the date changes. I need to practise using the same expressions with different details.

---

## 精编 45：Working Together from Different Cities
### 不同城市里的共同工作

**工作协作 · 访谈 · 进阶 · 约 372 词**

**学习目标**：识别远程协作中交接信息、时间口径和响应预期的重要性。

Interviewer: What surprised you about working with a team in several cities? Manager: I expected scheduling calls to be the hardest part. In fact, unclear handovers caused more delays. A person would finish work and send a file, but nobody knew whether the next person should review it, change it or simply keep it for reference. Distance exposed an ambiguity that already existed.

> 采访者：与多个城市的团队合作，最意外的是什么？经理：我原以为最难是约会议。实际上，不清楚的交接造成更多延误。有人完成工作发了文件，却没人知道下一位应该审阅、修改，还是仅供参考。距离暴露了原本就存在的模糊之处。

Interviewer: Can you give an example? Manager: A designer sent a proposal before going offline. The message said, "Please check." One colleague corrected the wording; another waited for a cost estimate; the designer expected a decision between two versions. Everyone had responded to a different interpretation. The document was not missing, but the decision attached to it was.

> 采访者：能举例吗？经理：设计师下线前发了方案，写着“请看看”。一位同事改措辞，另一位等成本估算，设计师却期待大家在两个版本中作选择。每个人回应的都是不同理解。文件没缺，缺的是与文件对应的决策要求。

Interviewer: What does a handover include now? Manager: We spell out the current state, the requested action, the person responsible and the point at which work can continue. We also say what does not need reviewing. That last detail prevents a settled choice from being reopened every time someone sees the document for the first time. A short message with clear boundaries beats a long message with none.

> 采访者：现在交接写什么？经理：明确当前状态、请求的行动、负责人，以及满足什么条件后能继续。也说明哪些不用再审。这能避免已决定事项每次被第一次看文件的人重新打开。有清楚边界的短消息，比没有边界的长消息更好。

Interviewer: How do you handle time zones? Manager: For important deadlines, we write the date, local time and time zone together. "Tomorrow afternoon" depends on the reader's location and when they open the message. We do not assume that a calendar invitation has removed every misunderstanding. A quick read-back is still useful when travel or a release depends on the time.

> 采访者：怎么处理时区？经理：重要截止时间把日期、当地时刻和时区写在一起。“明天下午”取决于读者在哪里、什么时候打开消息。我们不认为日历邀请能消除所有误会。涉及旅行或发布时，简单复述核对仍有价值。

Interviewer: Must people reply immediately? Manager: Not by default. Routine updates can wait until the next working period. If we are waiting on a specific answer, the message says so and gives a realistic deadline. We have a separate route for urgent problems. Without that distinction, every notification looks equally important, and people either interrupt everything or ignore too much.

> 采访者：必须立即回复吗？经理：默认不需要。常规更新可以等到下一个工作时段。如果正在等某个答复，消息会明确写出，并给合理期限。紧急问题另有渠道。没有这种区分，每个通知看起来都一样重要，大家要么不断被打断，要么忽略太多。

Interviewer: Has written communication replaced conversation? Manager: No. We still talk when the problem is uncertain or people disagree about what the words mean. Afterwards, we write down the decision. The point is not to force every interaction into a document. It is to let the next person act without having to reconstruct a conversation they were not present for.

> 采访者：文字沟通替代了谈话吗？经理：没有。问题不确定，或大家对词义理解不同时，仍然交谈，之后再记下决定。目的不是把一切互动塞进文档，而是让下一位不用重建一场自己没参加的谈话，也能采取行动。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| handovers | 工作交接 | handover 是名词；hand over a task 是动词短语。 |
| by default | 默认情况下 | 不等于不可例外。 |
| waiting on | 等待某人或某事 | wait on / wait for；此处指等待依赖项。 |
| spell out | 明确说明 | 不只是隐含在上下文。 |

#### 理解检查

1. What was missing from the first handover?
   - A. The expected decision and who would make it.
   - B. A longer greeting.
   - C. A colourful heading.

2. How were response expectations clarified?
   - A. By separating urgent requests from routine updates and stating deadlines with time zones.
   - B. By expecting everyone to stay online all night.
   - C. By removing every deadline.

**参考答案**：1. A — 有文件但没有说明需要谁作什么决定。；2. A — 把紧急与常规请求分开，并明确截止时刻及时区。

#### 复述
说明跨城协作的两个具体误会，以及改进后的交接包含什么。

#### 换条件表达
写一句交接：请对方北京时间周三下午三点前确认金额，无需重审排版。

参考表达（不唯一）：Please confirm the amount by three p.m. Beijing time on Wednesday. The layout does not need another review.

---

## 精编 46：A Repairer Who Sometimes Says No
### 有时会拒绝维修的修理师

**人物与生活 · 访谈 · 中级 · 约 347 词**

**学习目标**：理解维修沟通中的现象、原因不确定性、报价和授权边界。

Interviewer: What do customers usually say when they arrive? Repairer: Often, "It doesn't work." That is a useful beginning, but it covers many different things. Does the device fail to turn on, stop after a minute or make an unfamiliar sound? I ask what happened before the problem appeared and which actions they have already tried.

> 采访者：顾客到店通常说什么？修理师：常说“它坏了”。这是有用的开始，却涵盖很多不同情况。是无法开机，开一分钟就停，还是发出陌生声音？我会问故障前发生什么，以及已经尝试了哪些操作。

Interviewer: Can you identify the cause from that description? Repairer: Sometimes I recognise a familiar pattern, but I do not want to present a guess as a diagnosis of the machine. Similar symptoms can have different causes. I explain what I can check without opening it and what requires permission to take apart. The customer should understand the next step.

> 采访者：凭描述能确定原因吗？修理师：有时能认出熟悉的表现，但不想把猜测当成对机器的确诊。相似现象可能有不同原因。我会说明哪些检查不用拆机，哪些需要许可才能拆开，让顾客理解下一步。

Interviewer: How do you discuss the price? Repairer: I separate the inspection from the repair. If I need to find out which component has failed, that work takes time even when the customer later decides against replacing it. Once I have an estimate, I explain the parts, labour and any uncertainty. A single number without a scope creates arguments later.

> 采访者：怎样谈价格？修理师：把检查和维修分开。查明哪个部件失效需要时间，即使顾客之后决定不更换也是如此。有了估价，我会解释零件、人工和不确定项。只给一个数字、不说范围，之后容易争执。

Interviewer: What if the problem becomes larger? Repairer: I stop and call before I go ahead. The original permission does not mean permission for any cost I discover. I describe what changed and ask whether the customer wants another estimate. Some decide to collect the device as it is. That choice belongs to them, not to me.

> 采访者：发现问题比预期大怎么办？修理师：先停下，打电话再继续。最初的同意，不代表允许我发现多少费用就做多少。我说明变化，问要不要新的估价。有些人决定按现状取回设备，那是他们的选择，不是我的。

Interviewer: Do you ever recommend not repairing something? Repairer: Yes. A part may be unavailable, or I may be unable to offer a dependable result. Something can also be repairable without being worth doing for that particular owner. They might value it for personal reasons, so I ask rather than assuming that replacement is always preferable.

> 采访者：会建议不要修吗？修理师：会。有时买不到零件，或无法提供可靠结果。也有东西虽然能修，对某位所有者却未必值得。他们也可能因个人感情重视它，所以我会问，而不假定更换总是更好。

Interviewer: What makes a good handover? Repairer: Showing what was tested, explaining any limits and letting the customer try the device. I keep the account specific. "It is fixed" sounds reassuring, but "the charging connection passed this test" tells them more. If another fault remains, it needs a name rather than a place in the small print.

> 采访者：好的交付是什么样？修理师：展示检查了什么，说明局限，让顾客试用。我尽量具体。“修好了”听起来安心，但“充电接口通过了这项测试”信息更多。如果还有别的故障，需要明确指出，而不是藏在小字里。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| take apart | 拆开 | take it apart 代词放中间。 |
| find out | 查明 | 先检查再确认原因。 |
| go ahead | 继续进行 | 这里指得到同意后动工。 |
| worth doing | 值得做 | worth 后用 doing。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why does the repairer avoid giving a definite cause immediately?
   - A. Similar symptoms may have different causes.
   - B. The customer is always wrong.
   - C. The repairer never examines devices.

2. What must happen before extra work begins?
   - A. The customer receives the revised estimate and agrees.
   - B. The old estimate is silently replaced.
   - C. The device is sold.

**参考答案**：1. A — 相似现象可能来自不同原因，需检查后判断。；2. A — 费用或范围变化要告知并获得同意。

#### 复述
说明从客户描述到检查、报价、确认和交付的顺序，并指出不能维修时怎样说明。

#### 换条件表达
你是修理师：检查费二十美元，正式维修另报价，得到同意后才做。

参考表达（不唯一）：The inspection costs twenty dollars. I’ll give you a separate repair estimate and only go ahead after you agree.

---

## 精编 47：The Guide Who Leaves Time to Get Lost
### 会给迷路留时间的导游

**旅行出行 · 访谈 · 中级 · 约 363 词**

**学习目标**：从访谈提取路线设计的实际约束，练习时间、距离和集合说明。

Interviewer: How do you begin planning a walking tour? Guide: I start with the people, not a list of famous places. How far can they comfortably walk? Are they interested in buildings, food or stories? A route that looks efficient on a map may feel exhausting in the rain. The same distance can be a different experience for different groups.

> 采访者：你从哪里开始设计步行路线？导游：从人开始，不是从名胜清单开始。他们能舒服地走多远？对建筑、美食还是故事感兴趣？地图上高效的路线，下雨时可能令人疲惫。同样的距离，对不同人会是不同体验。

Interviewer: Why do you leave gaps in the schedule? Guide: Because a real walk is not a line moving across a screen. People stop to tie shoes, read a window display or ask a question. I leave time for those moments. If we arrive early, there is something nearby to explore. If every minute is already promised, a small delay becomes everyone's problem.

> 采访者：为什么行程留空？导游：真实步行不是屏幕上一条线移动。有人系鞋带、看橱窗、问问题。我会给这些时刻留时间。提前到，还能看看附近；若每分钟都已承诺，一个小延误就会变成全队的问题。

Interviewer: How do you keep together in a busy street? Guide: Before we enter, I explain where we will stop and which side of the road we will use. I do not give a long direction while walking ahead with my back to people. At crossings, I wait until everyone has arrived before beginning the next explanation.

> 采访者：热闹街道上怎样保持队伍不散？导游：进入前先说明停在哪里、走哪一侧。我不会背对大家走在前面时讲长长的路线指引。过路口后，等所有人到齐再开始下一段讲解。

Interviewer: What do you tell people during free time? Guide: A location and a time they can identify without me. "Get back to the square later" is too vague. I might say, "Meet under the clock beside the library entrance by four fifteen at the latest." Then I ask someone to repeat the place and time, especially if the group is large.

> 采访者：自由活动时交代什么？导游：即使没我在，他们也能识别的地点和时间。“晚点回广场”太模糊。我会说：“最晚四点十五，在图书馆入口旁的钟下集合。”然后请人复述，队伍大时尤其如此。

Interviewer: What if a visitor wants to do something outside the route? Guide: I explain the trade-off. The market may be possible, but it could mean missing the museum. I cannot promise both if the opening times conflict. Sometimes we split into smaller groups with clear plans. Choice is useful only when the person understands what each option involves.

> 采访者：有人想去路线外的地方怎么办？导游：解释取舍。市场也许能去，但可能错过博物馆。如果开放时间冲突，不能两个都保证。有时分成小组，各自安排明确。知道每个选项包含什么，选择才有意义。

Interviewer: What do visitors remember afterwards? Guide: Often a small encounter I could not have scheduled: a shopkeeper explaining a recipe or someone asking about a doorway. The route makes those encounters possible, but it should not squeeze them out. I want people to leave knowing a little more about a place, not simply knowing that they completed my list.

> 采访者：游客后来记住什么？导游：往往是我无法提前安排的小相遇，比如店主解释食谱，或有人问起某个门口。路线让相遇成为可能，却不该把它挤掉。我希望游客多了解一个地方，而不仅仅知道完成了我的清单。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| leave time for | 为……留时间 | leave time for a break。 |
| keep together | 保持在一起 | 用于队伍不走散。 |
| get back to | 返回 | get back to the meeting point。 |
| at the latest | 最迟 | 常在具体时刻后使用。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why does the guide not fill every minute?
   - A. Stops and different walking speeds need room.
   - B. The guide has no plan.
   - C. Visitors must shop at every stop.

2. What makes the meeting point useful?
   - A. A precise visible landmark and a clear return time.
   - B. Only the name of a large district.
   - C. A message saying come back later.

**参考答案**：1. A — 实际步速、休息和意外需要余量。；2. A — 地点和时刻都要让游客能实际识别和执行。

#### 复述
为第一次来访的人设计三站路线，说明步行距离、休息和集合点。

#### 换条件表达
告诉队员最晚四点十五回到图书馆正门旁的蓝色钟下。

参考表达（不唯一）：Please get back by four fifteen at the latest. We’ll meet under the blue clock beside the library’s main entrance.

---

## 精编 48：Making a Small Exhibition
### 办一场小展览

**文化与艺术 · 访谈 · 中级 · 约 353 词**

**学习目标**：理解作品选择、观众路径和说明文字之间的联系。

Interviewer: How did you choose photographs for the exhibition? Curator: We began with a question about how people use the neighbourhood after dark. There were beautiful photographs we had to leave out because they did not help with that question. Choosing the strongest individual image was not always the same as choosing the image that helped the group make sense.

> 采访者：怎样为展览选照片？策展人：先有一个问题：天黑后，人们怎样使用社区。有些漂亮照片仍要舍弃，因为与问题无关。单张最强的照片，不一定最能帮助整组作品形成清楚意思。

Interviewer: Do captions explain what viewers should feel? Curator: I try not to. A caption can identify a place, give context or draw attention to a detail that is easy to miss. It should not tell everyone that the same photograph is definitely hopeful or sad. A description and an instruction about emotion are different kinds of writing.

> 采访者：说明文字告诉观众应该感受什么吗？策展人：我尽量避免。说明可以标出地点、补充背景，或提醒容易忽略的细节，但不该告诉所有人某张照片一定充满希望或悲伤。描述与指挥感受是两种不同写法。

Interviewer: What changed after you tested the room? Curator: Our first visitor entered from the side and began with what we considered the final photograph. We had assumed the route was obvious. We moved a panel and made the opening question visible from both doors. The room had to explain its organisation without somebody guiding each person through it.

> 采访者：试布展后改了什么？策展人：第一位观众从侧门进来，先看了我们认为应该最后看的照片。原以为路线很明显，于是移动展板，让两个门口都能看见开场问题。房间必须在没人逐个带领的情况下，说明自己的组织方式。

Interviewer: Can every photograph stand on its own? Curator: Some can. Others need a relationship with the next image. A closed shop looks different beside a picture of its morning delivery. Together, they raise a question about time rather than abandonment. I want those connections to be available, but not so forced that the images become illustrations of my explanation.

> 采访者：每张照片都能独立成立吗？策展人：有些可以，有些需要与旁边的照片关联。一家关门的店，放在它清晨收货的照片旁，意义会不同。组合提出的是时间问题，不一定是荒废。我希望关联可被发现，却不强迫图片变成我解释的插图。

Interviewer: How do you know whether the text is working? Curator: I ask visitors what they thought a sentence meant. Asking whether they liked it is not enough. One caption came across as blaming local residents, although that was not our intention. We changed it to describe the observed behaviour and removed a claim we could not support.

> 采访者：怎么知道文字是否有效？策展人：问观众觉得某句话是什么意思，只问喜不喜欢不够。有一段说明给人的印象像在责备居民，尽管本意不是如此。我们改成描述实际观察到的行为，删掉一项无法支持的主张。

Interviewer: What makes a small exhibition successful? Curator: Not that visitors repeat the exact interpretation we planned. I hope they leave with a detail they can describe and a question they want to explore. Last week, two neighbours disagreed about a photograph and stayed to compare memories. That conversation was part of what the exhibition made room for.

> 采访者：小展览怎样算成功？策展人：不是观众逐字复述预定解释。我希望他们带走一个能描述的细节和一个想继续探索的问题。上周两位邻居对一张照片有不同理解，留下来比较各自记忆。展览也为这样的谈话留出空间。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| draw attention to | 让人留意 | 注意说明要突出的细节。 |
| leave out | 舍弃；省去 | 选择本身构成叙事。 |
| stand on its own | 独立成立 | 无需额外背景也能被理解。 |
| came across | 给人某种印象 | come / came / come；此处不是偶遇。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why were some strong photographs left out?
   - A. They did not support the exhibition’s central question.
   - B. There was a rule against colour.
   - C. They were all damaged.

2. What did the first visitor reveal?
   - A. The intended starting point was not obvious.
   - B. Every caption was too short.
   - C. The exhibition had no lights.

**参考答案**：1. A — 单张好看不代表适合共同主题。；2. A — 观众的实际入口和观看顺序不同于策展人的假设。

#### 复述
说明策展人怎样从一组作品形成一个可被观众理解的展览。

#### 换条件表达
建议删掉一段解释，把观众注意力引向照片中的一处具体细节。

参考表达（不唯一）：Could we shorten this caption and draw attention to the open door in the background?

---

## 精编 49：The Person at the Other End of the Call
### 电话另一端的人

**工作协作 · 访谈 · 中级 · 约 367 词**

**学习目标**：从服务访谈中提取问题描述、承诺范围与下一次联系安排。

Interviewer: What is your first task when someone calls about a problem? Adviser: To understand which problem they want us to deal with. A late parcel, an incorrect item and an unexpected charge may all arrive in the same angry sentence. I let the person finish, then repeat the main points and ask which consequence needs attention first.

> 采访者：有人打电话反映问题时，你的首要任务是什么？客服：理解他希望我们处理哪个问题。包裹晚到、货不对、收费意外，可能在一句气话里同时出现。我会让对方说完，再复述重点，问哪个后果最需要先处理。

Interviewer: Does repeating it back make the call longer? Adviser: Sometimes by a minute, but it can prevent us from investigating the wrong issue. Last week, I thought a customer wanted a refund. What she needed most was confirmation that a replacement would arrive before an event. The money still mattered, but the deadline changed which options were useful.

> 采访者：复述会延长通话吗？客服：有时多一分钟，却能避免查错问题。上周我以为一位顾客要退款，她最需要的其实是确认替换品能否在活动前到达。钱仍重要，但期限改变了哪些选项有用。

Interviewer: What do you say when you do not know the answer? Adviser: I name what I need to check and who has that information. I do not say "It should be fine" just to end an uncomfortable pause. A guess can become the promise a customer plans around. It is better to explain the uncertainty while still taking responsibility for the next contact.

> 采访者：不知道答案时怎么说？客服：说明要查什么、谁掌握信息。我不会只为结束尴尬沉默就说“应该没问题”。猜测可能变成顾客据以安排的承诺。更好的做法是说明不确定，同时对下一次联系负责。

Interviewer: How do you keep someone informed without a final solution? Adviser: I separate an update from a resolution. I can say what has been checked, what remains open and when I will get back to them. If I promise a call by four, the call must happen even if the repair is unfinished. Silence does not become useful because the news is incomplete.

> 采访者：没有最终解决方案，怎样让顾客知情？客服：把进展和解决分开。我能说查过什么、什么仍未决、什么时候回复。如果承诺四点前打电话，即使没修完也应该打。消息不完整，不代表沉默就有用。

Interviewer: Are phrases such as "as soon as possible" helpful? Adviser: They can express intention, but they do not tell a customer when to expect anything. Where I can make a specific commitment, I do. Where I cannot, I explain the dependency and arrange a review time. The aim is a promise I can keep, not the most comforting sentence available.

> 采访者：“尽快”这类话有帮助吗？客服：能表达意愿，却没有告诉顾客何时期待结果。能具体承诺就具体说；不能时说明依赖条件，并约定再次检查的时间。目标是能兑现的承诺，不是选一句最令人安心的话。

Interviewer: What do you record after the call? Adviser: The agreed action, the customer's stated need and the next contact time. I avoid writing only that the customer was difficult or unhappy. A colleague taking over needs enough information to act, not just an impression of the conversation. Good notes let the customer continue without starting the entire story again.

> 采访者：通话后记什么？客服：已商定行动、顾客明确需求、下次联系时间。我避免只写“顾客难沟通”或“不满意”。接手同事需要足够行动的信息，而不只是对谈话的印象。好记录让顾客继续处理，而不必重讲全部故事。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| deal with | 处理 | deal / dealt / dealt。 |
| keep someone informed | 让某人持续知情 | 不等于承诺已经解决。 |
| get back to | 回复 | get back to someone by a time。 |
| as soon as | 一……就…… | 不要用它代替具体可承诺期限。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why does the adviser repeat the problem back?
   - A. To check that the customer and adviser mean the same thing.
   - B. To fill the silence.
   - C. To avoid recording anything.

2. What can an update honestly contain when the repair is unfinished?
   - A. What was checked, what remains open and when contact will happen next.
   - B. A false promise that it is fixed.
   - C. Only an apology with no next step.

**参考答案**：1. A — 复述用于核对理解，防止解决错误的问题。；2. A — 进展更新不必假装完成，但应说清已知、未决和下一联系时间。

#### 复述
设计一次服务回访，包含已核实事实、未解决事项和明确联系时间。

#### 换条件表达
说明你不能承诺今天修好，但会在下午四点前更新检查结果。

参考表达（不唯一）：I can’t promise it will be repaired today, but I’ll get back to you with the inspection result by four this afternoon.

---

## 精编 50：A Garden between Two Buildings
### 两栋楼之间的花园

**城市与环境 · 访谈 · 中级 · 约 342 词**

**学习目标**：解释共享空间中的目标差异、试行安排与维护责任。

Interviewer: How did the garden begin? Organiser: The space between our buildings was mostly bare paving. Some residents wanted flowers; others wanted somewhere to sit. A few worried about noise and extra work. We did not begin by promising a large garden. We asked whether three containers and a bench would be a useful first experiment.

> 采访者：花园怎样开始的？组织者：两栋楼之间原本大多是空铺地。有居民想种花，有人想有地方坐，也有人担心噪声和新增工作。我们没有一开始就承诺大花园，而是先问三个种植箱加一张长椅，是否值得试试。

Interviewer: Who made the decisions? Organiser: We invited people who used the courtyard at different times, including those who did not want to garden. A clear route to the doors mattered to everyone. We marked the proposed positions and checked whether a pushchair could pass. A plan seen from above had missed how people actually moved through the space.

> 采访者：谁来决定？组织者：我们邀请不同时间使用院子的人，也包括不想种植的人。通往各门口的路线畅通，对大家都重要。先标位置，再检查婴儿车能否通过。俯视图上的计划，漏掉了人们实际怎样穿过空间。

Interviewer: What happened after you set up the containers? Organiser: At first, everyone helped. During the first holiday, nobody was clearly in charge of watering. Each person thought another neighbour would do it. Several plants dried out. The problem was not a lack of enthusiasm at the opening; it was the absence of an ordinary routine afterwards.

> 采访者：种植箱放好后怎样？组织者：起初人人帮忙。第一次假期时，却没人明确负责浇水，每个人都以为其他邻居会做，几株植物干枯了。问题不是开场缺热情，而是之后没有日常安排。

Interviewer: How did you change that? Organiser: We agreed to take turns and added a simple handover. If someone was away, they needed another person to confirm the exchange rather than leaving a message nobody answered. We had to work around different schedules. A routine that depended on the same person being available every evening would not last.

> 采访者：怎么改？组织者：轮值并加简单交接。有人不在，需要另一位确认接班，而不是留条没人回复的消息。我们得适应不同日程。若安排依赖同一个人每晚都有空，就持续不了。

Interviewer: Did everyone become a gardener? Organiser: No, and that was not necessary. One neighbour kept the seat clean. Another helped translate the notices. Some simply used the bench and let us know when a path was blocked. Participation could take different forms without making every person responsible for every task.

> 采访者：人人都成了园丁吗？组织者：没有，也没必要。一位邻居清洁座椅，另一位翻译通知。有人只是坐坐，并在通道被挡时告诉我们。参与可以有不同方式，不必让每个人对每项任务都负责。

Interviewer: Will you expand it? Organiser: We will review the first season before deciding. We want to know which plants survived, who did the work and whether people still felt welcome to use the courtyard. A bigger space would be worth considering only if we understood how to care for it. More containers would not automatically mean a better shared place.

> 采访者：会扩大吗？组织者：先复盘第一个季节，再决定。我们想知道哪些植物活下来，谁承担了工作，大家是否仍觉得院子欢迎自己。只有理解怎样照料，才值得考虑更大规模。种植箱更多，不会自动让共享空间更好。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| in charge of | 负责 | 说明具体职责，不只是职位。 |
| take turns | 轮流 | take turns watering。 |
| set up | 建立；布置 | set / set / set。 |
| work around | 围绕限制调整 | work around different schedules。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did the group begin with a few containers?
   - A. To test the work and responsibilities before expanding.
   - B. Because nobody liked plants.
   - C. Because the whole courtyard was already a farm.

2. What problem appeared during the first holiday?
   - A. Watering responsibility was assumed rather than assigned.
   - B. Every resident moved away.
   - C. There was no gate.

**参考答案**：1. A — 小范围试行能观察实际维护工作和责任。；2. A — 大家以为别人会浇水，直到明确轮值和交接才改善。

#### 复述
说明花园最初的提议、不同居民的需求、试行和维护办法。

#### 换条件表达
为共享书架提出小范围试行，并写清谁负责整理。

参考表达（不唯一）：Let’s set up one shelf for a month. We’ll take turns checking it each Friday before deciding whether to add another.

---

## 精编 51：The Parcel for the Day Before
### 早到一天的包裹

**日常生活 · 叙事 · 基础 · 约 343 词**

**学习目标**：核对实际日期、预期日期和交付地点，清楚提出可执行请求。

The delivery message said my desk would arrive on Wednesday between nine and twelve. I arranged to work from home that morning. On Tuesday, while I was on a train, another message appeared: "Your driver is nearby." The desk was a day early, and nobody was at home to receive it.

> 送货消息说，书桌周三九点到十二点送达。我因此安排那天上午在家工作。周二，我正在火车上，另一条消息出现了：“司机即将到达。”书桌提前一天来了，家里却没人收货。

I tried to call the number in the message, but the call did not connect. Then the driver rang me. I explained that the agreed day was Wednesday. He could not wait until I returned. I asked whether he could leave it with the building's reception desk, but first I needed to check that they could accept such a large box.

> 我拨打消息里的号码，没接通。随后司机打来电话。我解释约定是周三，他不能等我回家。我问能否交给大楼前台，但需要先确认前台能否接收这么大的箱子。

Reception said there was not enough space. The driver could not simply leave the desk in the entrance because it would block the door. We agreed that he would return it to the local depot. I wrote down the depot's name, but I still did not know when I could collect it or whether another delivery was possible.

> 前台说空间不够。司机也不能直接放在入口，因为会挡门。我们同意先运回本地配送点。我记下配送点名称，但还不知道什么时候能取，或者能否重新配送。

That evening, I got in touch with the delivery company through the order page. I included the order number and the original Wednesday message. I did not want to tell the story again without the dates. The adviser checked the record and offered either another delivery on Friday or collection from the depot the next afternoon.

> 当晚，我通过订单页面联系配送公司，附上订单号和原先约定周三的消息。我不想又重讲一遍却没有具体日期。客服查记录后给两个选择：周五再送，或第二天下午去配送点自取。

My sister had a car large enough for the box and could help on Wednesday. Before choosing collection, I asked the adviser to confirm the address, opening time and what I needed to bring. I also checked the box dimensions with my sister. A plan to pick it up would not help if it did not fit in the car.

> 姐姐的车足够大，周三也能帮忙。选择自取前，我请客服确认地址、开放时间和需要带什么，还与姐姐核对箱子尺寸。如果放不进车里，计划自取也没有意义。

We collected the desk at three on Wednesday. At home, I compared the label on the box with the order and checked the contents before putting it together. The problem had begun with a single changed day. Solving it required a new place, a new time and a person who had actually agreed to help.

> 周三三点，我们取到书桌。回家后，我把箱上标签与订单核对，检查箱内物品再组装。问题起于送货日期改变一天，解决它却需要新的地点、时间，以及一个确实答应帮忙的人。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| a day early | 提前一天 | early 表示早于预定时间。 |
| leave it with | 把它交给……保管 | 明确接收人或地点。 |
| got in touch | 取得了联系 | get / got；get in touch with someone。 |
| pick it up | 取件 | 代词 it 在 pick 与 up 之间。 |

#### 理解检查

1. What was wrong with the delivery?
   - A. It arrived a day earlier than the agreed day.
   - B. The parcel was empty.
   - C. The customer ordered nothing.

2. What did the narrator ask the company to confirm?
   - A. The new collection point and its opening time.
   - B. The driver’s favourite route.
   - C. A free holiday.

**参考答案**：1. A — 包裹比约定早一天送达，当时家里没人。；2. A — 最后需要明确在哪里取、什么时候开放。

#### 复述
讲清原约定、提前送达造成的问题，以及最后怎样取到包裹。

#### 换条件表达
说明周二没人收货，请改到周三，或经确认交给前台。

参考表达（不唯一）：Nobody will be home on Tuesday. Could you deliver it on Wednesday, or leave it with reception if they agree?

---

## 精编 52：Two Rooms, One Moving List
### 两个房间，一份搬家清单

**日常生活 · 叙事 · 基础 · 约 345 词**

**学习目标**：描述物品位置、搬运顺序与尺寸限制，区分愿望与可行方案。

My new flat had two rooms and much less storage than my old one. I knew I could not take everything, so I began with a list of furniture rather than a list of boxes. I measured the bed, desk and shelves. Then I measured the new rooms, including the doors and the narrow space behind the entrance.

> 新公寓有两个房间，储物空间比旧家少很多。我知道不能全搬，于是先列家具，而不是先列纸箱。我量床、书桌和书架，再量新房间，包括门口和入口后面狭窄的位置。

The desk would fit against the bedroom wall, but it would not fit through the doorway as one piece. I had almost missed that detail because the room itself was large enough. A friend helped me take apart the legs and label the screws. We put the small parts in a bag and attached it to the desk.

> 书桌靠卧室墙放得下，却不能整张穿过门口。我差点漏掉这个细节，因为房间本身够大。朋友帮我拆下桌腿，给螺丝作标记，再把小零件装袋固定在桌子上。

I labelled each box with its destination and a short description. "Bedroom: winter clothes" was more useful than "things." On two sides, I wrote the label again so it would remain visible in the van. Books went into small boxes. I could carry one large box of cushions, but not one equally large box of books.

> 每箱都写目的房间和简短内容。“卧室：冬装”比“杂物”有用。我在箱子两侧都写标签，方便在货车里看见。书装小箱：一大箱靠垫搬得动，同样大的书箱却搬不动。

I set aside one box for the first evening: a towel, clean clothes, a cup, a plate, the kettle and my phone charger. It went into the car last and came out first. I kept documents and keys with me. That way, I did not need to search through the whole move before making a drink or finding a toothbrush.

> 我单独留了一箱首晚用品：毛巾、干净衣服、杯子、盘子、水壶和手机充电器。它最后上车，最先搬出。证件和钥匙随身带着。这样，不用翻遍搬家物品，才能喝水或找牙刷。

At the new flat, a friend started stacking boxes in the hallway. I asked him to leave a clear path and put each one near its room instead. Nothing heavy went on top of the box marked fragile. We put the bed together before arranging the shelves. I wanted somewhere to sleep more than I wanted a finished living room.

> 到新家后，朋友开始把箱子堆在走廊。我请他留出通道，把箱子放在各自房间附近。重物不放在标着易碎的箱子上。我们先装床，再整理书架。相比布置好的客厅，我更需要能睡觉的地方。

That night, half the boxes were still closed. The desk top rested beside the wall, waiting for the next day. Yet I could wash, make tea and go to bed without moving a pile of belongings. The new place did not look complete, but the parts of an ordinary evening were already possible.

> 那晚，一半箱子还没打开，桌面靠墙等第二天再装。但我能洗漱、泡茶、上床睡觉，不用先搬开一堆东西。新家看起来还没布置完，普通夜晚所需的事情却已经做得到。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| fit through | 能够穿过 | 尺寸与空间相容。 |
| take apart | 拆开 | take / took / taken。 |
| set aside | 单独留出 | 为第一晚用品留出一箱。 |
| on top of | 在……上面 | 用于位置或堆放关系。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why could the desk not be moved as one piece?
   - A. It could not fit through the new doorway.
   - B. It had been sold.
   - C. The van was empty.

2. What was in the box kept separate?
   - A. Things needed on the first night.
   - B. Only old magazines.
   - C. Tools that nobody could use.

**参考答案**：1. A — 目的地门口尺寸限制使整张搬运不可行。；2. A — 把首晚所需用品单独放，避免到新家后翻找。

#### 复述
按准备、发现问题、调整和第一晚四部分复述搬家，使用三个位置介词。

#### 换条件表达
告诉搬运者把写着厨房的箱子放在餐桌旁，别压在易碎箱上。

参考表达（不唯一）：Please put the box marked kitchen beside the dining table, not on top of the fragile box.

---

## 精编 53：A Weekend Plan with a Wet-Weather Version
### 带雨天备选的周末计划

**旅行出行 · 叙事 · 基础 · 约 329 词**

**学习目标**：比较两个方案并说明触发条件，让行程随实际约束调整。

Four of us wanted to spend Saturday together. The first idea was a long walk by the coast, followed by lunch outside. We all liked it, but two people were coming by train, and the weather forecast kept changing. Before buying tickets, we agreed that the day needed more than one possible version.

> 我们四个人想周六一起过。最初提议沿海长距离散步，再在户外午餐。大家都喜欢，但有两个人坐火车来，天气预报也不断变化。买票前，我们同意这一天应该不止一种安排。

The coast plan would depend on fairly dry weather. The walk began twenty minutes from the station and took about two hours. If it rained halfway through, there was no quick bus back. We wrote down those details instead of describing the plan only as "an easy walk." Easy meant different things to each person.

> 海边方案需要天气比较干燥。起点离车站二十分钟，步行大约两小时。中途下雨，也没有很快返回的公交车。我们把这些具体情况写下来，不只说“轻松散步”。每个人理解的“轻松”并不一样。

For a backup plan, we found a museum close to the town station. The journey was shorter, and a café nearby could seat all four of us. We checked the opening hours and whether tickets needed booking. We also checked the outdoor restaurant's cancellation conditions before promising to eat there.

> 备用方案是镇上车站附近的博物馆。路程更短，旁边咖啡馆能坐下四个人。我们查了开放时间和是否需要订票，也先了解户外餐厅的取消条件，再决定要不要订那里。

On Friday evening, we compared the two costs in the group chat. The museum ticket added a little, but the cheaper train almost balanced it. Everyone could afford either option. We decided to make up our minds at eight on Saturday morning, before the two travellers left home. Until then, nobody needed to guess which ticket to buy.

> 周五晚上，我们在群里比较费用。博物馆要多花一点门票，但较便宜的火车票几乎抵消了差额。两个方案大家都能接受。我们约定周六早上八点决定，赶在两位朋友出门前，让他们不用猜该买哪种票。

Saturday brought heavy rain. We sent one clear message confirming the museum, the train and the meeting point. I packed a light jacket in case the afternoon improved, but nobody treated the coast walk as a secret second appointment. At the museum, we found a room of old maps that kept us talking for nearly an hour.

> 周六下大雨。我们发出一条明确消息，确认博物馆、车次和集合点。我带件轻外套，以防下午天气转好，但没人把海边散步偷偷当成另一项必须完成的安排。博物馆里的一间旧地图展厅，让我们聊了将近一小时。

By lunchtime, the rain had stopped. We took a short walk through the town rather than trying to recover the entire coastal route. We had changed the main activity but kept what mattered to the group: enough time together and a day nobody had to struggle through. The unused plan could wait for another weekend.

> 午饭时雨停了。我们在镇上短走一段，没有试图补完整条海岸路线。主要活动变了，但保留了大家在意的事情：有时间相处，不必勉强熬过一天。没用上的计划，可以等另一个周末。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| depend on | 取决于 | depend on the weather / transport。 |
| in case | 以防 | 不等于简单条件 if。 |
| a backup plan | 备用计划 | 说明何时采用。 |
| make up our minds | 作出决定 | make up one’s mind。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did the group not book the outdoor lunch immediately?
   - A. They wanted to confirm the weather and cancellation conditions.
   - B. They had already eaten.
   - C. Nobody wanted to travel.

2. What was the indoor alternative?
   - A. A museum near the station and lunch afterwards.
   - B. The same walk in heavy rain.
   - C. Staying at the closed café.

**参考答案**：1. A — 天气和预订条件未确认前，不急着作承诺。；2. A — 室内方案仍保留共同出行和午餐，只更换主要活动。

#### 复述
介绍两个方案的地点、交通、花费和决定时间，并说明雨天切换条件。

#### 换条件表达
给朋友写一条消息：周六上午确认天气，若下大雨就改去美术馆。

参考表达（不唯一）：Let’s check the forecast on Saturday morning. If there’s heavy rain, we’ll go to the gallery instead.

---

## 精编 54：Two Hours at the Station
### 车站里的两个小时

**旅行出行 · 叙事 · 中级 · 约 351 词**

**学习目标**：在信息不完整时比较等待和改道，区分预计时间与已确认时间。

The departure board changed while I was buying a bottle of water. My train, due to leave at three, was delayed because of a problem further along the line. The screen gave no new departure time. Around me, people looked from the board to their phones as though another glance might produce information that had not yet arrived.

> 我买水时，发车屏幕变了。原定三点的列车因前方线路问题延误，却没有新的发车时间。周围的人在屏幕和手机之间来回看，仿佛再看一眼就能得到尚未出现的信息。

My first thought was to find another route immediately. A journey planner suggested leaving from a different station, but the connection included two changes and a bus. I could see the first departure time more easily than the final arrival time. Buying a ticket quickly would feel like taking action, but I still needed to know what that action would achieve.

> 我首先想立刻找别的路线。行程工具建议去另一站，方案里有两次换乘和一段公交。我更容易看见第一班出发时间，却难看清最后到达时间。赶紧买票会让我感觉在行动，但仍需要知道行动能带来什么。

At the help desk, I asked for the best current estimate and whether my existing ticket could be used elsewhere. The assistant explained what was confirmed and what was still being checked. For the moment, waiting remained a reasonable option. I took a seat where I could see the board without standing in front of it.

> 在咨询台，我询问当前最可靠的预计时间，以及原票能否在其他线路使用。工作人员解释哪些已确认、哪些仍在核查。目前继续等仍是合理选择。我找了个能看见屏幕的座位，不再站到屏幕前。

Then I tried to get hold of the friend who was collecting me. She had planned to leave work early. I told her I might arrive around two hours late and asked her not to start driving yet. We agreed on another update at four, even if the railway had not given a final answer. She sent instructions for entering the flat if she was unavailable later.

> 随后，我联系准备接站的朋友。她原打算早下班。我说可能晚到两小时，请她先别开车。我们约好四点再更新一次，即使铁路还没给最后答复。她也发来稍晚不方便接站时，如何进入公寓的说明。

At four ten, the board announced a departure at four forty. I checked the platform and allowed time to reach it, but I did not tell my friend I had already left. When the train actually moved, I sent the arrival estimate shown onboard. That message separated a scheduled departure from a journey that was finally underway.

> 四点十分，屏幕公布四点四十分发车。我核对站台，留出走过去的时间，却没有告诉朋友“已经出发”。等列车实际开动，再发车上显示的预计到达时间。这条消息把计划发车与真正开始的行程分开了。

I arrived hungry and later than planned, but not outside a locked building with someone waiting at the wrong station. The hours had not been useful in the way I originally intended. Still, I had changed the arrangements around the delay instead of spending all of them refreshing the same uncertain screen.

> 我又饿又晚地到了，但没有被锁在楼外，也没有让人在错误时间去车站等。那几个小时没有按原计划发挥作用，但我至少调整了延误影响到的安排，而不是把时间全部用来刷新同一个不确定的屏幕。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| for the moment | 目前；暂时 | 此刻情况不代表最终安排。 |
| get hold of | 联系到 | get hold of a person。 |
| allowed time | 留出了时间 | allow time to do；将余量与后续任务联系。 |
| due to leave | 原定出发 | be due to do；不表示已保证发生。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did the narrator not immediately buy the alternative ticket?
   - A. The route and arrival benefit were not yet clear.
   - B. They had no destination.
   - C. The station sold no tickets.

2. What did contacting the host achieve?
   - A. Changed the collection arrangement before the narrator travelled.
   - B. Guaranteed that the railway would reopen.
   - C. Removed every delay.

**参考答案**：1. A — 转车路线的实际时间尚不明确，不能只比较表面发车时间。；2. A — 提前告知，让接站与门禁安排可以调整。

#### 复述
说明延误期间哪些信息确定、哪些只是预计，以及你会先联系谁。

#### 换条件表达
通知接待者你目前预计晚两小时，准确时间确定后再更新。

参考表达（不唯一）：At the moment, I expect to arrive about two hours late. I’ll update you when the arrival time is confirmed.

---

## 精编 55：My First Day on a Team That Spoke in Shortcuts
### 满是缩略语的入职第一天

**工作协作 · 叙事 · 中级 · 约 343 词**

**学习目标**：澄清团队术语，判断一句话要求的是信息、行动还是决定。

By lunchtime on my first day, I had a notebook full of letters. The team used short names for projects, documents and review stages. I could follow the general topic, but several sentences contained almost no ordinary nouns. Everyone else seemed comfortable, and I worried that asking too many questions would make the meeting about my confusion.

> 入职第一天午饭前，我已记下满页字母。团队用简称称呼项目、文档和审核阶段。我大概跟得上主题，但有些句子几乎没有普通名词。其他人都很自如，我担心问太多，会把会议变成专门处理我的困惑。

During a break, I asked a colleague what one abbreviation stood for. She gave me the full name. It was useful, but it did not explain why the team needed it by Friday or what I was supposed to contribute. I had translated the letters without understanding the work attached to them.

> 休息时，我问同事一个缩写代表什么。她说出全名，有帮助，却没有解释团队为何周五前需要它，或希望我贡献什么。我翻译了字母，仍没理解与之相连的工作。

In the next meeting, I tried a more specific question: "When you say the review is ready, do you mean it is ready for us to read, or that it has already been approved?" Two experienced colleagues gave different answers. The shortcut had hidden a disagreement that was not mine alone. We paused to clarify the state of the document.

> 下一次会议，我换了一个更具体的问题：“你说审核已准备好，是指可以让我们读，还是已经批准？”两位老同事给了不同答案。原来简称藏着分歧，并不只是我一个人不懂。大家停下来确认文档状态。

I continued to take notes, but I added an example beside each term. Who created this document? Who decided whether it was finished? What action followed the decision? When I could not answer, I marked a question rather than inventing an explanation. A colleague agreed to go through the list with me at the end of the week.

> 我继续记笔记，但在每个术语旁补例子：谁创建文档，谁决定是否完成，决定后做什么？回答不了就标问题，不编解释。一位同事同意周末前与我一起过一遍清单。

The list became a short guide for the next new starter. We did not remove the team's shortcuts; they were efficient once their meaning was shared. We added plain-language explanations and links to actual examples. We also separated terms that described a file from terms that described a decision, because mixing those had caused several mistakes.

> 清单后来成为下一位新人的简短指南。我们没有取消团队简称；一旦含义共享，它们确实高效。只是补上通俗解释和真实示例链接，并把表示文件的词与表示决策的词分开，因为混淆曾造成几次错误。

On Friday, I was asked to follow up on an unanswered question. I knew which person to contact and what reply would let the work continue. I still did not understand every abbreviation, but I could name what I needed to bring up. That felt more like joining the team than silently copying a longer sequence of letters.

> 周五，我被请去跟进一个未答复问题。我知道该联系谁，也知道什么答复能让工作继续。虽然仍不懂所有缩写，但能说清需要提出什么。这比默默抄下更长一串字母，更像真正加入团队。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| stood for | 代表；是……的缩写 | stand / stood / stood；What does it stand for? |
| follow up on | 跟进 | 对已有事项继续处理。 |
| take notes | 记笔记 | notes 常用复数。 |
| bring up | 提出话题 | bring / brought / brought。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why was knowing the full form of the abbreviation not enough?
   - A. The narrator still needed to know what action it required.
   - B. The letters were invisible.
   - C. The team never used words.

2. What made the glossary more useful?
   - A. Examples of decisions and actions beside the terms.
   - B. Only a longer alphabetical list.
   - C. Removing the original terms.

**参考答案**：1. A — 展开缩写只解释名称，没有解释在此处应做什么。；2. A — 术语与具体任务、例子关联，才能帮助行动。

#### 复述
描述新人遇到的语言障碍及其解决办法，区分术语定义和实际要求。

#### 换条件表达
在会议中确认：这个缩写我明白了，但你希望我批准还是只阅读？

参考表达（不唯一）：I understand what the abbreviation stands for, but do you need me to approve this or just read it?

---

## 精编 56：The Feedback That Changed One Paragraph
### 只改了一段的反馈

**工作协作 · 叙事 · 中级 · 约 348 词**

**学习目标**：把抽象评价转成具体修改，说明为什么改、怎样验证。

My proposal began with nearly a page about the history of our booking process. I had described the old spreadsheet, the growth in requests and several conversations with colleagues. I thought that giving the full background would make the recommendation convincing. The first reviewer wrote only one sentence at the top: "What decision do you need from me?"

> 我的方案开头用了将近一页介绍预订流程历史：旧表格、请求增长、与同事的几次谈话。我以为背景交代完整，建议就更有说服力。第一位审阅者只在顶端写了一句：“你需要我作什么决定？”

At first, I took the comment as a request to cut words. I shortened the introduction and removed two examples. The draft became half a page shorter, but the same question remained. I had still not said whether I wanted permission to investigate, money to buy a tool or approval to change the process immediately.

> 起初，我把这条反馈理解为删字。缩短引言，去掉两个例子，稿子少了半页，但问题仍在。我依然没说明要的是允许调查、买工具的预算，还是立即改变流程的批准。

I asked the reviewer to show me where she expected the answer. She pointed to the first paragraph. Before reading all the evidence, she needed to know what choice it supported. Getting to the point was not simply writing fewer sentences. It meant giving the reader a useful frame for the sentences that followed.

> 我请审阅者指出，她预期在哪里看到答案。她指着第一段：阅读全部证据前，需要知道证据支持什么选择。切入重点，不只是句子更少，而是先给读者一个理解后文的有效框架。

I rewrote the opening to spell out a two-week trial, the proposed cost and the person who would review the result. I added what would happen if we delayed: the team could continue with the current method, but would need temporary help during the busiest week. The background moved below the recommendation, where readers could inspect it if they needed more detail.

> 我重写开头，明确两周试行、拟议费用，以及谁评估结果。也补上延期后果：可以继续现有方式，但最忙那周需要临时人手。背景移到建议下方，供需要细节的读者查看。

Then I gave the revised paragraph to a colleague who had not attended our discussions. I asked her to tell me what she thought she was being asked to approve. She identified the trial correctly but assumed it included a permanent subscription. I changed one sentence to make clear that any ongoing purchase would require a separate decision.

> 接着，把修改后的段落给一位没参加讨论的同事，请她说说以为要批准什么。她正确识别了试行，却误以为包含长期订阅。我又改一句，说明持续采购需要另作决定。

We eventually approved the limited trial. The rest of the proposal still mattered, but the opening now did its job. I kept the review comments beside both versions. They reminded me that to act on feedback, I sometimes needed another conversation before another edit. "Clearer" was a useful direction only when we agreed on what the reader needed to understand.

> 最后，有限试行获得批准。方案其他内容仍重要，但开头终于完成了自己的任务。我把反馈与两个版本放在一起，提醒自己：落实反馈有时需要先再谈一次，才能再改一次。只有共同明确读者需要理解什么，“更清楚”才是有用方向。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| getting to the point | 切入重点 | get to the point；不是单纯缩短字数。 |
| spell out | 明确写出 | 补足决策所需信息。 |
| shorten | 缩短 | shorten the introduction；不要只删字而保留模糊要求。 |
| act on | 根据……采取行动 | act on feedback。 |

#### 理解检查

1. What did the first reviewer find unclear?
   - A. What decision the proposal requested.
   - B. The font colour only.
   - C. The author’s name.

2. Why was the revision more useful?
   - A. It stated the requested decision, cost and consequence of delay near the start.
   - B. It added more general history.
   - C. It removed every explanation.

**参考答案**：1. A — 读完背景仍不知道希望批准什么。；2. A — 改动集中在读者作决定所需信息，而非只追求更短。

#### 复述
先描述原稿结构，再说明反馈如何转成三个具体修改，最后解释验证方式。

#### 换条件表达
请同事指出“更清楚”究竟意味着补金额、补期限，还是改行动要求。

参考表达（不唯一）：When you say it needs to be clearer, do you mean the amount, the deadline or the action we’re requesting?

---

## 精编 57：Two Charts, One Small Change
### 两张图，同一个小变化

**科技与信息 · 分析叙事 · 进阶 · 约 371 词**

**学习目标**：检查图表中的尺度、分母、时间范围和样本量，准确口述变化。

The slide looked like good news. Two bars showed a rise in the share of customers who completed a form, from forty-eight percent to fifty percent. At first sight, the second bar seemed almost twice as tall. Someone described the result as a dramatic improvement before asking how the chart had been drawn. The numbers were printed correctly; the visual impression was another matter.

> 幻灯片看起来是好消息。两根柱子显示完成表单的客户比例从48%升到50%。乍看，第二根几乎高一倍。有人还没问图怎么画，就称之为显著改善。标出的数字没错，视觉印象却是另一回事。

The vertical axis did not start from zero. It began close to the lower value, which enlarged the visible difference between the bars. A restricted scale can sometimes reveal a small change, but it needs clear labelling and an appropriate chart design. Here, the bars encouraged people to compare their apparent heights as if those heights represented the whole amounts.

> 纵轴并非从零开始，而是从接近较低数值的位置起，放大了可见差距。限制尺度有时能展示细微变化，但需要清楚标注和合适图形。这次，柱状图让人把可见高度当成整体数量来比较。

We made a second version with the full percentage scale and placed the values beside it. The difference looked smaller, as the underlying numbers suggested. Neither redrawing the chart nor using a calmer colour answered the next question: was the change meaningful for the decision? To discuss that, we needed the number of customers and a consistent definition of completion.

> 我们做第二版，用完整百分比尺度，并在旁边列数值。差异看起来更小，符合底层数字。但重画图或换温和颜色，都没回答下一问：变化对决策是否有意义？为此，需要客户数量和一致的“完成”定义。

We also had to put the comparison into context. The first figure covered a full month; the second originally covered only the first week after a change. The mix of customers might differ. We did not declare that the result was false, but we stopped describing it as a settled comparison until we could define periods and groups that made sense together.

> 还得把比较放入背景。第一个数覆盖整月，第二个起初只覆盖调整后的第一周，客户构成可能不同。我们没有宣称结果是假的，但在明确可合理比较的期间和群体前，不再把它说成已经定论的比较。

When presenting the revised slide, I said the rate had risen by two percentage points, not that it had doubled. Relative to forty-eight percent, the increase was about four point two percent. Both descriptions could be calculated from the same pair of values, yet they answered different questions. I kept the arithmetic visible so listeners could follow the distinction.

> 展示改后的图时，我说提高了两个百分点，不是翻倍。相对于48%，增幅约4.2%。两种描述都能从同一对数值算出，却回答不同问题。我把计算写出来，让听众能理解区别。

The meeting ended with a request for a better comparison, not a celebration or a rejection of the change. We wanted the effect described in proportion to the evidence available. The first chart had made a modest movement look dramatic. The more useful version made it easier to ask what had actually changed and what we would need to know before acting on it.

> 会议结束时，大家要求更合适的比较，没有庆祝，也没有否定调整。我们希望描述的分量与现有证据相称。第一张图让小变化显得戏剧化，较有用的版本则让人更容易问：实际改变了什么，行动前还需要知道什么？

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| at first sight | 乍看之下 | 描述第一印象。 |
| start from | 从……开始 | 此处指纵轴起点。 |
| in proportion to | 相对于……的比例 | 帮助说明大小关系。 |
| put the comparison into context | 把比较放入背景 | 加入时间、范围和基准。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did the first chart make the change look dramatic?
   - A. Its vertical scale began close to the two values.
   - B. The underlying values were ten times larger.
   - C. The chart compared different currencies.

2. What additional information was requested?
   - A. Sample size and how the two periods were defined.
   - B. Only brighter colours.
   - C. The designer’s preferred font.

**参考答案**：1. A — 截短的纵轴放大了视觉差异，但原数值只从48%到50%。；2. A — 比例差异需要结合分母和时间口径理解。

#### 复述
用两种方式说明48%到50%的变化，明确区分百分点与相对百分比。

#### 换条件表达
向同事解释从20%到25%是增加5个百分点，相对增加25%。

参考表达（不唯一）：The rate rose by five percentage points, from twenty to twenty-five percent. That is a relative increase of twenty-five percent.

---

## 精编 58：The Cheaper Quote That Cost More
### 总价更高的便宜报价

**消费选择 · 决策叙事 · 进阶 · 约 379 词**

**学习目标**：在相同需求与时间范围下比较报价，辨认一次性费、持续费和范围差异。

Our community workshop needed a printer for six months. Supplier A quoted sixty dollars a month, while Supplier B quoted eighty. At the top of a comparison table, the choice appeared obvious. Several people preferred A before reading the attachments. The monthly figures were easy to compare because they shared the same unit, but they did not yet describe the same service.

> 社区工作坊需要租打印机六个月。供应商A每月六十美元，B每月八十。放在比较表顶端，选择似乎很明显。几个人没看附件就选A。月费单位相同，看起来容易比较，但描述的还不是同一种服务。

A's offer included the machine but charged one hundred and twenty dollars for setup and thirty dollars for each monthly delivery. B included setup and one delivery a month in its price. We needed that delivery schedule, so ignoring it would not be a harmless simplification. We could not compare like for like until we had identified the services we actually required.

> A含机器，安装另收一百二十美元，每月配送每次三十。B的价格包含安装和每月一次配送。我们确实需要这个配送频率，所以忽略它不是无害简化。先明确实际需要的服务，才能在同等条件下比较。

Over the course of six months, A's listed charges added up to six hundred and sixty dollars: three hundred and sixty in monthly fees, one hundred and twenty for setup and one hundred and eighty for deliveries. B's listed total was four hundred and eighty. Those figures concerned only the stated items in this fictional example; tax and any additional use still needed clarification.

> 六个月内，A列明的费用共六百六十美元：月费三百六十、安装一百二十、配送一百八十。B列明总额四百八十。这些数字只对应这个虚构例子中已列出的项目，税费和额外使用仍需确认。

We sent both suppliers the same questions. What volume was included? What happened if a delivery was missed? Could the machine be collected after six months without another period beginning automatically? The answers changed our view of convenience as well as price. A service that required a volunteer to solve each delivery problem could consume time that our table had not measured.

> 我们向两家发相同问题：包含多少用量，配送漏送怎么办，六个月后能否取走机器而不自动开始新一期？回答不仅影响价格判断，也影响便利性。若每次配送问题都需志愿者解决，就会耗费表中没计入的时间。

One person asked whether choosing B meant we no longer cared about cost. It was the opposite: we had finally agreed on which costs belonged in the decision. We also recorded conditions that were not included in either total. Where an answer remained uncertain, we kept a note instead of entering zero, which would have made missing information look like a free service.

> 有人问，选B是不是不在乎成本。恰恰相反：我们终于统一了哪些成本应纳入决定，也记下两个总额都没包含的条件。答案仍不确定时，保留备注，而不是填零，以免把缺信息伪装成免费。

We chose B after confirming the remaining terms in writing. The comparison table kept its first version beside the final one. It showed why our decision had changed without suggesting that a higher monthly price was always better. The useful habit was to add up a defined package over a defined period, then make the unresolved conditions visible to whoever would approve it.

> 书面确认剩余条款后，我们选了B。比较表保留第一版和最终版，说明决定为何改变，却不暗示月费高总是更好。有用的做法是：在明确期间内加总明确范围的项目，再让审批者看见尚未解决的条件。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| add up | 加起来 | add up the costs / add them up。 |
| included in | 包含在……之中 | be included in the total / price。 |
| over the course of | 在……期间 | 强调一整段时期。 |
| like for like | 同等条件下比较 | 避免把不同范围当作同一种方案。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why was the lower monthly figure insufficient for the comparison?
   - A. It left out setup and delivery fees.
   - B. It used no numbers.
   - C. It covered a different colour of paper only.

2. What did the team do before choosing?
   - A. Compared the same service over the same six-month period.
   - B. Multiplied only the larger price.
   - C. Chose whichever email arrived first.

**参考答案**：1. A — 报价范围不同，必须先补齐一次性和配送费用。；2. A — 统一需求和期间后再合计。

#### 复述
说明两个方案各包含什么，按六个月计算总额，并明确哪些条件仍需确认。

#### 换条件表达
核对服务费是否包含安装、培训和每月两次配送。

参考表达（不唯一）：Could you confirm whether setup, training and two deliveries per month are included in the quoted price?

---

## 精编 59：A Change of Date, Not a Cancellation
### 是改日期，不是取消

**工作协作 · 沟通叙事 · 中级 · 约 340 词**

**学习目标**：识别听者推断与说话者本意的差别，用复述确认行动。

My message to the venue was short: "We need to move next Tuesday's workshop. Please update the booking." I had not yet chosen a new date, but I expected the room to remain reserved while we discussed alternatives. Alex, the coordinator, read the message differently. He thought we wanted to call off the booking and start again later.

> 我发给场地方的消息很短：“下周二工作坊需要改期，请更新预订。”新日期还没定，我却期待讨论备选时房间继续保留。协调员亚历克斯理解不同：他以为我们要取消预订，以后再重新开始。

He released the room and sent a brief reply: "Updated as requested." I read that as confirmation of my own intention. Neither of us had stated the actual action in full. By Friday, another group had booked Tuesday, and I was asking whether we could put the event back by just two days. Thursday was available, but the equipment was not.

> 他释放房间，简短回复：“已按要求更新。”我把它读成对自己意图的确认。我们谁都没完整说出具体行动。到周五，另一组订走了周二；我问能否只推迟两天。周四有房，但设备没有。

At first, our emails focused on whose sentence had been clearer. That did not help us find a room or a projector. We switched to a call and described the sequence without arguing about motives. I explained what I had intended to keep. Alex explained what the booking system did when a reservation was cancelled.

> 起初，邮件都在争谁说得更清楚，但这找不到房间或投影仪。我们改为通话，先描述发生顺序，不争论动机。我说明原本想保留什么，亚历克斯解释系统取消预订后会发生什么。

That helped us clear up the misunderstanding. We found a smaller room on Thursday with equipment already installed. It meant changing the seating plan and reducing the number of extra chairs. I checked those changes with the workshop leader before accepting. A new date alone would not restore every part of the original arrangement.

> 这样才澄清误会。我们找到周四一间已装设备的小房间，需要改座位布局，减少备用椅子。接受前，我与工作坊负责人核对这些变化。仅有新日期，并不能恢复原安排的所有部分。

Alex's next message listed the details: the original Tuesday booking was cancelled, the Thursday room was confirmed, equipment was included, and catering still required my approval. I replied line by line. This was longer than "updated," but it let both of us identify exactly what had changed and what remained open.

> 亚历克斯下一封消息逐项列出：周二原预订已取消，周四房间已确认，含设备，餐饮仍需我批准。我也逐项回应。虽然比“已更新”长，却让我们都能识别究竟改了什么、什么仍未确定。

On Thursday, the workshop began as agreed. Afterwards, I kept a simple rule for change requests: state the old arrangement, the new arrangement if known, and the parts that must stay in place. I also ask the other person to confirm the action, not merely the receipt of my message. A read email is not necessarily a shared plan.

> 周四，工作坊按约开始。之后，我给变更请求留下一条简单规则：说明旧安排、已知的新安排，以及哪些必须保留。我也请对方确认行动，而不只是确认收到了消息。邮件已读，并不必然代表计划已共享。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| call off | 取消 | 不同于 postpone 推迟。 |
| put the event back | 推迟活动 | put something back；也可用 postpone。 |
| clear up | 澄清 | clear up a misunderstanding。 |
| as agreed | 按约定 | 须有双方确实一致的安排。 |

#### 理解检查

1. What did Alex incorrectly assume?
   - A. That moving the event meant cancelling the room booking entirely.
   - B. That the room was blue.
   - C. That the customer wanted an earlier start.

2. What did the new confirmation include?
   - A. The old date, new date and what would remain booked.
   - B. Only the word changed.
   - C. A longer apology without dates.

**参考答案**：1. A — 把改期理解成取消全部预订，导致房间被释放。；2. A — 同时说明变更项和保留项，避免对行动范围的误解。

#### 复述
用两个视角复述同一封消息：发件人希望什么、收件人做了什么，再说明澄清办法。

#### 换条件表达
明确告诉同事：周二的会议改到周四，但请保留设备和餐饮预订。

参考表达（不唯一）：We’re moving Tuesday’s meeting to Thursday. Please keep the equipment and catering bookings and change their dates too.

---

## 精编 60：When the Training Video Would Not Play
### 培训视频无法播放时

**科技与信息 · 问题解决 · 中级 · 约 347 词**

**学习目标**：按可观察现象排查问题，记录尝试及其结果，避免同时改变多个条件。

Five minutes before the training session, the video froze on the opening image. The sound continued, but the picture did not move. I was sharing my screen with twelve colleagues. My first instinct was to click several settings at once, turn everything off and start again. That might help, but it would also make it harder to explain what had changed.

> 培训前五分钟，视频停在开场画面。声音继续，图像不动。我正向十二位同事共享屏幕。第一反应是同时点几个设置，全部关闭再来。但即使有效，也难解释究竟什么改变了结果。

I asked the group to pause and described exactly what I could observe. Someone suggested testing the file without screen sharing. The picture still froze. Another short video played normally on the same machine. That did not prove the cause, but it helped us work out that the problem might be connected with this particular file rather than every video.

> 我请大家暂停，先准确描述观察到的现象。有人建议不共享屏幕，直接播文件，画面仍停住。同一电脑上的另一段短视频正常播放。这没有证明原因，却帮助限定：问题可能与这个文件有关，而不是所有视频都不能播。

We checked one thing at a time. A second browser produced the same result as well. A colleague downloaded another copy, which also froze at the same point. I wrote down each attempt and its outcome. "Tried everything" would have sounded thorough, but it would not help the person who later had to inspect the file.

> 我们每次只检查一项。换浏览器后结果也相同，另一位同事重新下载一份，也停在同一处。我记下每次尝试和结果。“什么都试过了”听起来全面，却帮不了之后检查文件的人。

The instructor had a transcript and still images showing the main steps. We used those to get around the immediate obstacle and continued the discussion. Before moving on, I told the group that this was an alternative format for the session, not a confirmed repair. Anyone who needed the moving demonstration would receive another update.

> 讲师有文字稿和展示主要步骤的静态图。我们用它们暂时绕过障碍，继续讨论。继续前，我说明这是本次课程的替代形式，不代表视频已修好。需要动态演示的人，之后会收到更新。

After the session, I sent the support team the file name, the point where the picture stopped and the list of tests. I also explained that the audio played normally and other files worked. They did not need another description of how frustrating the session had been; they needed a reproducible account of what happened.

> 课后，我把文件名、停止位置和检查清单发给支持团队，也说明声音正常、其他文件能播。他们不需要再听一次课程多令人沮丧，而是需要能复现问题的记录。

The next day, the team supplied a new copy. I tested the troublesome section and then the complete video before sharing it. In the follow-up message, I said what I had checked and asked people to report if their device behaved differently. We could finally provide the promised demonstration without confusing a useful temporary route with a finished solution.

> 第二天，团队提供新副本。我先测原先出问题的片段，再测完整视频，之后才分享。跟进消息中，我说明检查范围，请大家报告自己设备上的不同表现。终于能提供原先承诺的演示，也没有把有用的临时办法与彻底解决混为一谈。

#### 文章表达

| 表达 | 中文 | 用法 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| work out | 弄清；解决 | work out what is happening。 |
| one thing at a time | 每次只检查一项 | 让每次尝试的结果更容易解释。 |
| as well | 也；同样 | 在此类叙述中常用于补充另一个观察。 |
| get around | 绕过障碍 | 临时替代不等于根本解决。 |

#### 理解检查

1. Why did the team test another short video?
   - A. To distinguish a problem with one file from a broader playback issue.
   - B. To make the meeting longer.
   - C. To erase the original file.

2. Why was the temporary transcript not called a complete fix?
   - A. People could continue learning, but video playback remained unresolved.
   - B. Nobody could read it.
   - C. It replaced the entire course permanently.

**参考答案**：1. A — 比较其他文件有助于限定问题范围。；2. A — 替代材料让学习继续，但原播放问题还在。

#### 复述
说明问题、三次检查及结果、临时替代和交接信息，别把试过当成修好。

#### 换条件表达
向支持团队报告：声音能播放，但画面不动；换过浏览器后仍相同。

参考表达（不唯一）：The audio plays, but the picture is frozen. I’ve tried another browser, and the result is the same.

---

## 历史原稿档案

下列文字为原有 20 篇文章，保留其原始学习对象。精编阅读以此前 40 篇为主。

## 历史原稿 1：A Typical Day in My Life
### 我的日常生活

The alarm clock rings at six thirty in the morning. I slowly open my eyes and stretch my arms above my head. The sunlight streams through the curtains, casting a warm glow across my bedroom. I take a deep breath and prepare myself for another busy day.

> 闹钟在早上六点半响起。我慢慢睁开眼睛，把双臂伸过头顶。阳光透过窗帘照进来，在卧室里投下温暖的光芒。我深吸一口气，为又一个忙碌的日子做好准备。

After getting out of bed, I immediately head to the bathroom to wash my face and brush my teeth. The cold water helps me wake up completely. I look at myself in the mirror and smile, ready to face whatever challenges the day might bring.

> 起床后，我立刻去浴室洗脸刷牙。冷水帮我完全清醒过来。我看着镜子里的自己微笑着，准备好迎接这一天可能带来的任何挑战。

Breakfast is the most important meal of the day, as my mother always says. I prepare scrambled eggs with toast and a cup of freshly brewed coffee. The aroma fills the small kitchen, making me feel hungry. While eating, I check my phone for any important messages and glance through the news headlines.

> 早餐是一天中最重要的一餐，正如我母亲常说的那样。我准备了炒蛋配吐司和一杯现煮咖啡。香味弥漫在小厨房里，让我感到饿了。吃饭时，我查看手机上是否有重要信息，并浏览新闻标题。

By seven forty-five, I am dressed and ready to leave. I grab my briefcase, put on my coat, and step out into the crisp morning air. The weather is pleasant today, with clear blue skies and a gentle breeze. I decide to walk to the subway station instead of taking the bus, as it gives me some exercise.

> 七点四十五分，我穿好衣服准备出门。我拿起公文包，穿上外套，走进清晨凉爽的空气中。今天天气宜人，天空晴朗，微风习习。我决定步行去地铁站而不是坐公交车，这样可以锻炼身体。

The subway is crowded as usual during rush hour. People are packed tightly together, some reading newspapers, others listening to music through their headphones. I manage to find a seat near the window and take out my book to read during the twenty-minute journey.

> 地铁在高峰时段一如既往地拥挤。人们紧紧地挤在一起，有的在看报纸，有的戴着耳机听音乐。我设法在窗边找到一个座位，拿出书来阅读这二十分钟的车程。

When I arrive at my office building, I greet the security guard and take the elevator to the fifteenth floor. My colleagues are already at their desks, typing away on their computers. I sit down at my workstation, turn on my computer, and begin checking my emails.

> 当我到达办公楼时，我向保安问好，乘电梯到十五楼。同事们已经在办公桌前敲打电脑了。我坐在自己的工位上，打开电脑，开始查看邮件。

The morning passes quickly with meetings and phone calls. I have a presentation to prepare for next week, so I spend most of my time researching and organizing data. My boss stops by my desk around eleven o'clock to discuss the project timeline and offer some suggestions.

> 上午在会议和电话中很快过去了。我有一个下周要准备的演示，所以大部分时间都在研究和整理数据。我的老板大约十一点钟来到我的办公桌前，讨论项目时间表并提供一些建议。

Lunchtime arrives, and I join my coworkers at a nearby restaurant. We order sandwiches and salads, chatting about our weekend plans and sharing funny stories. It is nice to take a break from work and relax with friends. The conversation helps relieve the stress of the morning.

> 午餐时间到了，我和同事们一起去附近的餐厅。我们点了三明治和沙拉，聊着周末计划，分享有趣的故事。从工作中休息一下，和朋友放松一下真好。聊天有助于缓解上午的压力。

The afternoon is devoted to completing reports and attending a training session. The training is about new software that our company has recently adopted. I find it interesting and take detailed notes. Learning new skills is always beneficial for career development.

> 下午用于完成报告和参加培训。培训是关于我们公司最近采用的新软件。我觉得很有趣，做了详细的笔记。学习新技能对职业发展总是有益的。

By five o'clock, I have finished most of my tasks. I organize my desk, make a to-do list for tomorrow, and say goodbye to my colleagues. The elevator is crowded again as everyone leaves at the same time. I wait patiently for the next one.

> 到五点钟，我已经完成了大部分任务。我整理办公桌，列出明天的待办事项，向同事们道别。电梯又挤满了人，因为大家都在同一时间离开。我耐心地等待下一班。

The journey home is more relaxed than the morning commute. I listen to a podcast about personal development and think about what I learned today. The train is less crowded, and I even manage to get a seat.

> 回家的路程比早上的通勤更轻松。我听着关于个人发展的播客，思考今天学到的东西。火车不那么拥挤了，我甚至找到了一个座位。

When I reach home, I change into comfortable clothes and prepare a simple dinner. Tonight, I make pasta with tomato sauce and vegetables. Cooking is therapeutic for me, allowing my mind to rest after a long day. I eat while watching the evening news on television.

> 到家后，我换上舒适的衣服，准备一顿简单的晚餐。今晚，我做了番茄酱蔬菜意面。做饭对我来说是一种放松，让我在漫长的一天后让大脑休息。我边吃边看电视上的晚间新闻。

After dinner, I wash the dishes and tidy up the living room. Then I spend an hour reading a novel that I have been meaning to finish. Reading before bed helps me relax and improves my vocabulary. I make a note of any interesting words I encounter.

> 晚饭后，我洗碗并整理客厅。然后我花一个小时读一本我一直想读完的小说。睡前阅读帮助我放松并提高词汇量。我会记下遇到的任何有趣的单词。

Before going to sleep, I reflect on my day. I think about what went well and what I could improve. I set my alarm clock for tomorrow and turn off the lights. As I close my eyes, I feel grateful for another productive day. Tomorrow will bring new opportunities and challenges, and I am ready to embrace them.

> 睡觉前，我反思这一天。我思考哪些做得好，哪些可以改进。我设好明天的闹钟，关灯。当我闭上眼睛时，我对又一个富有成效的一天心存感激。明天将带来新的机遇和挑战，我已经准备好拥抱它们。

## 历史原稿 2：The Modern Workplace
### 现代职场

The modern workplace has undergone significant changes over the past few decades. Technology has transformed how we communicate, collaborate, and conduct business. Companies now operate in a global environment, with employees working from different locations and time zones.

> 现代职场在过去几十年中经历了重大变化。技术改变了我们沟通、协作和开展业务的方式。公司现在在全球环境中运营，员工在不同的地点和时区工作。

In today's office, computers and smartphones are essential tools. Employees use email, instant messaging, and video conferencing to stay connected with colleagues and clients. Cloud computing allows teams to share documents and work on projects simultaneously, regardless of their physical location.

> 在今天的办公室里，电脑和智能手机是必不可少的工具。员工使用电子邮件、即时通讯和视频会议与同事和客户保持联系。云计算允许团队共享文档并同时处理项目，无论他们身在何处。

The traditional nine-to-five schedule is becoming less common. Many companies now offer flexible working hours, allowing employees to choose when they start and finish their workday. This flexibility helps people balance their professional and personal lives more effectively.

> 传统的朝九晚五工作时间正变得越来越少见。许多公司现在提供弹性工作时间，允许员工选择何时开始和结束工作日。这种灵活性帮助人们更有效地平衡职业和个人生活。

Remote work has become increasingly popular, especially after recent global events. Employees can work from home, coffee shops, or anywhere with an internet connection. This arrangement reduces commuting time and expenses while providing a comfortable work environment.

> 远程工作变得越来越受欢迎，尤其是在最近的全球事件之后。员工可以在家、咖啡店或任何有互联网连接的地方工作。这种安排减少了通勤时间和费用，同时提供了舒适的工作环境。

However, remote work also presents challenges. Some people find it difficult to separate work from personal life when their home is also their office. Distractions such as household chores and family members can affect productivity. Companies must establish clear guidelines and provide support for remote workers.

> 然而，远程工作也带来了挑战。有些人发现当家也是办公室时，很难将工作与个人生活分开。家务和家庭成员等干扰因素会影响生产力。公司必须建立明确的指导方针并为远程工作者提供支持。

Open office layouts have replaced traditional cubicles in many organizations. These spaces encourage collaboration and communication among team members. However, some employees find open offices noisy and distracting, preferring private spaces for focused work.

> 开放式办公布局已经在许多组织中取代了传统的隔间。这些空间鼓励团队成员之间的协作和沟通。然而，一些员工发现开放式办公室嘈杂且分散注意力，更喜欢私人空间进行专注工作。

Teamwork is crucial in the modern workplace. Projects often require input from multiple departments, including marketing, finance, and engineering. Effective collaboration depends on clear communication, mutual respect, and shared goals. Team-building activities help strengthen relationships among colleagues.

> 团队合作在现代职场中至关重要。项目通常需要多个部门的投入，包括市场、财务和工程。有效的协作依赖于清晰的沟通、相互尊重和共同目标。团队建设活动有助于加强同事之间的关系。

Professional development is a priority for many employees. Companies invest in training programs, workshops, and conferences to help their staff acquire new skills. Continuous learning is essential in a rapidly changing job market, where new technologies emerge constantly.

> 职业发展是许多员工的首要任务。公司投资于培训项目、研讨会和会议，帮助员工获得新技能。持续学习在快速变化的就业市场中至关重要，新技术不断涌现。

Diversity and inclusion have become important values in the workplace. Organizations recognize that diverse teams bring different perspectives and ideas, leading to better decision-making and innovation. Companies implement policies to ensure equal opportunities for all employees, regardless of gender, race, or background.

> 多样性和包容性已成为职场的重要价值观。组织认识到多元化团队带来不同的观点和想法，从而带来更好的决策和创新。公司实施政策确保所有员工享有平等机会，无论性别、种族或背景如何。

Workplace culture significantly impacts employee satisfaction and retention. A positive culture fosters engagement, motivation, and loyalty. Leaders play a crucial role in shaping culture through their actions, communication, and decisions. Regular feedback and recognition help employees feel valued and appreciated.

> 职场文化对员工满意度和留任率有重大影响。积极的文化促进参与、动力和忠诚。领导者在通过行动、沟通和决策塑造文化方面发挥着关键作用。定期反馈和认可帮助员工感到被重视和欣赏。

Stress management is an important skill in today's fast-paced work environment. Deadlines, targets, and expectations can create pressure. Employees must learn to prioritize tasks, delegate when appropriate, and take breaks to maintain their well-being. Many companies offer wellness programs to support mental and physical health.

> 压力管理是当今快节奏工作环境中的一项重要技能。截止日期、目标和期望会造成压力。员工必须学会确定任务优先级、适当委派并休息以保持身心健康。许多公司提供健康计划来支持身心健康。

Networking is essential for career advancement. Building relationships with colleagues, clients, and industry professionals opens doors to new opportunities. Attending conferences, joining professional associations, and participating in online forums are effective ways to expand one's network.

> 社交网络对职业发展至关重要。与同事、客户和行业专业人士建立关系为新机会打开大门。参加会议、加入专业协会和参与在线论坛是扩展人脉的有效方式。

Leadership styles have evolved over time. Modern leaders emphasize empowerment, transparency, and servant leadership rather than command and control. They inspire and motivate their teams, provide guidance and support, and lead by example. Effective leaders listen to feedback and adapt their approach as needed.

> 领导风格随着时间的推移而演变。现代领导者强调赋能、透明和服务型领导，而不是命令和控制。他们激励和激发团队，提供指导和支持，以身作则。有效的领导者倾听反馈并根据需要调整方法。

The gig economy has created new opportunities for independent workers. Freelancers, consultants, and contractors offer their services to multiple clients, enjoying flexibility and variety. However, they also face challenges such as income instability and lack of benefits.

> 零工经济为独立工作者创造了新机会。自由职业者、顾问和承包商为多个客户提供服务，享受灵活性和多样性。然而，他们也面临收入不稳定和缺乏福利等挑战。

Looking ahead, the workplace will continue to evolve. Artificial intelligence and automation will change the nature of many jobs, requiring workers to adapt and learn new skills. Companies must embrace change and innovation to remain competitive in a dynamic global market.

> 展望未来，职场将继续演变。人工智能和自动化将改变许多工作的性质，要求工人适应并学习新技能。公司必须拥抱变化和创新，以在动态的全球市场中保持竞争力。

## 历史原稿 3：Shopping and Consumer Culture
### 购物与消费文化

Shopping has become an integral part of modern life, influencing our economy, society, and personal habits. From traditional markets to online platforms, the way we purchase goods has evolved dramatically over the years. Consumer culture shapes our identities, aspirations, and social relationships.

> 购物已成为现代生活的重要组成部分，影响着我们的经济、社会和个人习惯。从传统市场到在线平台，我们购买商品的方式多年来发生了巨大变化。消费文化塑造了我们的身份、愿望和社会关系。

Department stores and shopping malls were once the primary destinations for shoppers. These large retail spaces offered a wide variety of products under one roof, from clothing and electronics to home goods and groceries. People would spend hours browsing through stores, comparing prices, and making purchases.

> 百货商店和购物中心曾经是购物者的主要目的地。这些大型零售空间在一个屋檐下提供各种产品，从服装和电子产品到家居用品和杂货。人们会花几个小时在商店里浏览、比较价格并购买。

The rise of e-commerce has revolutionized the retail industry. Online shopping platforms like Amazon, eBay, and Alibaba allow consumers to browse millions of products from the comfort of their homes. With just a few clicks, items are delivered to doorsteps within days or even hours. This convenience has changed consumer expectations and behaviors.

> 电子商务的兴起彻底改变了零售业。亚马逊、eBay和阿里巴巴等在线购物平台允许消费者在家中舒适地浏览数百万种产品。只需点击几下，商品就会在几天甚至几小时内送到家门口。这种便利改变了消费者的期望和行为。

Price comparison websites and apps help shoppers find the best deals. Consumers can easily compare prices across multiple retailers, read product reviews, and access discount codes. This transparency has increased competition among sellers, forcing them to offer better prices and services.

> 价格比较网站和应用程序帮助购物者找到最优惠的交易。消费者可以轻松比较多个零售商的价格、阅读产品评论并获取折扣码。这种透明度增加了卖家之间的竞争，迫使他们提供更好的价格和服务。

Social media has become a powerful marketing tool for brands. Influencers promote products to their followers, creating trends and driving sales. User-generated content, such as reviews and unboxing videos, helps consumers make informed purchasing decisions. Brands invest heavily in digital marketing to reach their target audiences.

> 社交媒体已成为品牌的强大营销工具。网红向他们的粉丝推广产品，创造趋势并推动销售。用户生成的内容，如评论和开箱视频，帮助消费者做出明智的购买决定。品牌大力投资数字营销以触达目标受众。

Sustainable and ethical shopping has gained popularity in recent years. Consumers are increasingly concerned about the environmental and social impact of their purchases. They seek products made from sustainable materials, produced under fair labor conditions, and packaged in eco-friendly materials. Companies respond by adopting responsible practices and obtaining certifications.

> 可持续和道德购物近年来获得了普及。消费者越来越关注他们购买的环境和社会影响。他们寻求由可持续材料制成、在公平劳动条件下生产、使用环保材料包装的产品。公司通过采用负责任的做法和获得认证来回应。

The concept of minimalism challenges traditional consumer culture. Minimalists intentionally reduce their possessions, focusing on what truly adds value to their lives. This lifestyle emphasizes quality over quantity, experiences over material goods, and intentionality over impulse buying.

> 极简主义的概念挑战了传统的消费文化。极简主义者有意减少他们的物品，专注于真正为生活增加价值的东西。这种生活方式强调质量胜于数量，体验胜于物质商品，有意识胜于冲动购买。

Seasonal sales and promotional events drive consumer spending. Black Friday, Cyber Monday, and end-of-season sales attract millions of shoppers seeking discounts. Retailers create a sense of urgency through limited-time offers and exclusive deals, encouraging consumers to make purchases they might otherwise delay.

> 季节性销售和促销活动推动消费者支出。黑色星期五、网络星期一和季末销售吸引数百万寻求折扣的购物者。零售商通过限时优惠和独家交易创造紧迫感，鼓励消费者购买他们可能会推迟的东西。

Brand loyalty plays a significant role in consumer behavior. People develop preferences for certain brands based on quality, reputation, and emotional connections. Companies invest in building strong brand identities and customer relationships to encourage repeat purchases and word-of-mouth recommendations.

> 品牌忠诚度在消费者行为中起着重要作用。人们基于质量、声誉和情感联系对某些品牌产生偏好。公司投资于建立强大的品牌形象和客户关系，以鼓励重复购买和口碑推荐。

The psychology of shopping reveals interesting insights into consumer behavior. Factors such as store layout, music, lighting, and product placement influence purchasing decisions. Retailers use these techniques to create pleasant shopping experiences and encourage spending.

> 购物心理学揭示了消费者行为的有趣见解。商店布局、音乐、照明和产品摆放等因素影响购买决定。零售商使用这些技术创造愉快的购物体验并鼓励消费。

Personal finance experts advise consumers to shop wisely. Creating budgets, distinguishing between needs and wants, and avoiding impulse purchases help maintain financial health. Credit cards and buy-now-pay-later services can lead to debt if not used responsibly.

> 个人理财专家建议消费者明智购物。制定预算、区分需求和欲望、避免冲动购买有助于保持财务健康。如果不负责任地使用，信用卡和先买后付服务可能导致债务。

The future of shopping involves emerging technologies. Virtual reality allows customers to try on clothes virtually, while augmented reality helps visualize furniture in their homes. Artificial intelligence provides personalized recommendations based on browsing history and preferences. These innovations enhance the shopping experience and blur the line between online and offline retail.

> 购物的未来涉及新兴技术。虚拟现实允许顾客虚拟试穿衣服，而增强现实帮助在家具在家中可视化。人工智能根据浏览历史和偏好提供个性化推荐。这些创新增强了购物体验，模糊了在线和离线零售之间的界限。

Local businesses face challenges competing with large corporations and online giants. However, many consumers value the personal service, unique products, and community connection that small businesses provide. Supporting local shops helps sustain local economies and preserve neighborhood character.

> 本地企业面临与大公司和在线巨头竞争的挑战。然而，许多消费者重视小企业提供的个人服务、独特产品和社区联系。支持本地商店有助于维持本地经济并保护社区特色。
